# Progress log

One entry per story, newest last. Format:

```
## YYYY-MM-DD ARV-nnn <title>
- Summary:
- Files:
- Gates: backend PASS, e2e PASS, ...
- Security review: PASS (CWEs checked: ...)
- Learnings for later stories:
```

## 2026-10-01 Scaffold (before ARV-001)
- Summary: Repository created by mirroring AMAN's layout; security baseline (default deny, headers, limits, rate limiting, ProblemDetails, trusted proxies, CORS allowlist); security gate with 40 rules mapped to 14 CWEs; unit, API end-to-end and Playwright functional tests for the skeleton; wiki; harness (CLAUDE.md files, subagents, commands, skills, hooks, MCP servers); Phase 0 backlog.
- Gates run in the build environment (no NuGet access): every non-test project builds with 0 warnings; security gate 0 errors (6 warnings: health probe allowlist entries awaiting approval); e2e 58 of 58 pass; unit tests compiled and run against stand-ins only, so ARV-001 must run them for real.
- Security review: baseline controls in place for CWE-862, CWE-306, CWE-120, CWE-79 (CSP), CWE-269 (non-root images and pod security context); the rest arrive with their stories.
- Learnings: Playwright on the build machine needed an explicit Chromium path; default deny answers 401 even for unknown routes (by design: the fallback policy runs before routing).

## 2026-10-01 ARV-001 Prove the build on GitHub
- Summary: The repository moved to GitHub (private) with ci, security-scan, images, codeql (off until enabled), base-images and dev-environment workflows; Dependabot; workflows post failures as PR comments because job logs are not readable with the token scope in use.
- Gates: backend PASS, unit PASS, e2e PASS, security PASS (GitHub Actions).
- Learnings: the build container has no NuGet access; CI is the compile gate. Playwright had one flaky failure on PR #15 that passed on rerun.

## 2026-10-01 ARV-002 Chart hardening, image pins and CVE fixes (PR #15)
- Summary: read-only root filesystems with emptyDirs, TLS on every ingress, prd render refuses floating tags, chart render tests in CI; base images pinned by digest; Microsoft packages 10.0.1 to 10.0.12 and MessagePack 3.1.4 to 3.1.7 (CVE-2026-48109, -48502, -48506, -45591); nginx 1.28 (Debian, 123 high or critical) to 1.30.5 on Alpine; OS upgrades in image builds (libssl3t64 CVE-2026-84782).
- Gates: chart tests PASS, Trivy fs and image PASS, backend PASS, e2e PASS. Not passing yet: start under the chart security context on the dev cluster (human release).
- Security review: CWE-269 (non-root, read-only root, dropped capabilities), supply chain pins.
- Learnings: AMAN pins the same vulnerable MessagePack and SignalR MessagePack versions; nginx 1.28 images are no longer rebuilt.

## 2026-10-01 ARV-003 Local development environment (PR #16)
- Summary: docker-compose.dev.yml (TimescaleDB HA image, Kafka KRaft, Redis with password, smtp4dev) on 127.0.0.1 ports away from AMAN's; dev-up writes random passwords and appsettings.local.json; dev-check proves each dependency.
- Gates: dev-environment workflow PASS.

## 2026-10-01 ARV-004 Domain foundations (PR #17)
- Summary: EntityBase, auditable and soft-deletable bases, IDomain, IEvent with partition key, EventBase, ISvc markers, trimmed ICurrentUser (no access token), BaseCriteria with UTC range rules; Result, PagedData, Search and Fx.Specification from Fluentx 6.1.9.
- Gates: backend PASS (unit tests for ids, equality, hash stability, events, audit, soft delete, hash keys, specifications).
- Learnings: transient entities must not compare equal and the hash code must stay stable across save; AMAN got both wrong.

## 2026-10-01 ARV-005 NHibernate storage provider and unit of work (PR #18)
- Summary: async IStorageProvider and IUnitOfWork; convention mapper with snake_case singular names, quoted reserved words, version 7 ids, UTC timestamptz, enums by name; audit interceptor and soft delete listener; domain events handed to IDomainEventOutbox inside the transaction (fails closed until ARV-020); ExecuteSqlAsync takes [ConstantExpected] SQL with CA1857 as an error.
- Gates: backend PASS, integration PASS (Testcontainers.PostgreSql 4.15.0, TimescaleDB image), security PASS. Correction: the first CI runs of this PR reported green while the build and unit tests failed, because "dotnet ... | tee" ran under bash -e without pipefail; the workflows now run with pipefail and the unit tests pass for real.
- Security review: CWE-89 (constant SQL enforced at compile time, parameters bound), CWE-532 (no SQL parameter logging).
- Learnings: passing an IType instance to the by-code mapper breaks enum mapping (use the generic EnumStringType); NHibernate needs System.Configuration.ConfigurationManager at runtime; a plain NpgsqlDriver with Npgsql 10 handles UTC timestamptz without the legacy switch.

## 2026-10-01 ARV-006 Versioned SQL script runner and database roles (PR #19)
- Summary: embedded scripts applied in order under an advisory lock, one transaction per script with its schema_version row; drift and pending checks in the runner and at host startup; checksums.lock; 0001_roles.sql with ariva_migration and ariva_runtime and a safe login function; Ariva.Api.Main --migrate run by a Helm hook Job.
- Gates: backend PASS, integration PASS, security PASS. Not yet run: the Helm Job on a cluster.
- Security review: CWE-269 (runtime login DML only, cannot change schema or schema_version), CWE-89 (login function quotes with format %I and %L).
- Learnings: CA2100 accepts a switch over string literals but not a tuple switch; checksums must normalise CRLF for Windows checkouts.

## 2026-10-01 ARV-007 Logging and telemetry with secret redaction (PR #20)
- Summary: Serilog in every API host (JSON console, Loki and OTLP by configuration); a redaction enricher added in code removes sensitive properties, header dictionaries, bearer and basic credentials, JWTs and token query values; Microsoft.AspNetCore.Hosting fixed at Warning; traces and metrics over OTLP when an endpoint is set.
- Gates: backend PASS (268 unit, 15 integration), e2e PASS including the log scan (canary credentials sent by the suite never reach a host log).
- Security review: CWE-532.
- Learnings: CI now posts test counts and fails when a suite runs no tests; Application:Name placeholders are not resolved yet, so ArivaLogging resolves the one used for service names.

## 2026-10-01 ARV-008 Caching and Data Protection key ring (PR #21)
- Summary: FusionCache (AMAN entry options) with tags, Redis distributed level and backplane on one connection, memory-only without Redis; Data Protection keys in PostgreSQL (script 0002, append-only for the runtime role) encrypted with a certificate from the ariva-dataprotection secret, previous certificates for rotation, development certificate on vm-local only.
- Gates: backend PASS (275 unit, 18 integration including two-host key ring and Redis backplane), e2e PASS.
- Security review: CWE-287 (shared key ring protected at rest), CWE-611 (XML read without DTDs).
- Learnings: JSON booleans read back as "True" through IConfiguration; compare with GetValue<bool>.

## 2026-10-01 ARV-009 Permission model and permission matrix (PR #22)
- Summary: Global.Defaults.Permissions (Entity.Action, five actions per entity), [Permission] as an AuthorizeAttribute with on-demand policies that fail closed, role seed from the wiki 01 role descriptions, security/permission-matrix.json checked in-process for every host and by E2E for anonymous callers.
- Gates: backend PASS (306 unit), e2e PASS.
- Security review: CWE-862, CWE-863 (no role strings in controllers; audit read-only for every role); CWE-200 (product version for administrators only).

## 2026-10-01 ARV-010a Password login, lockout and token issuance (PR #23)
- Summary: User and UserRole (script 0003), PBKDF2-SHA256 600k with rehash, password policy with a bundled breached list and context words, NFKC usernames; one 401 for every sign-in failure; lockout 10 for 15 minutes counted in one SQL statement; ES256 at+jwt tokens with kid signed only by Ariva.Api.Main and validated by every host; permissions from stored grants; pending scope for temporary passwords; administrator unlock; token key secrets in the chart with a rule that keeps the signing key in api-main.
- Gates: backend PASS (393 unit, 29 integration with 1 skipped), e2e PASS against PostgreSQL with per-run random accounts and a DML-only runtime login, security PASS with one warning (SEC-052 for /api/auth/login, PENDING approval).
- Security review: CWE-287, CWE-307 (lockout and rate limit), CWE-204 and CWE-208 (uniform failure after a full hash check), CWE-916 (hash cost), CWE-347 (ES256 only, typ, kid), CWE-598 (no query string tokens), CWE-532 (passwords and tokens covered by the E2E log scan).
- Learnings: positional records need validation attributes on the constructor parameters, not with property:, or MVC throws on every request; a harness that compiles several projects as one assembly hides missing usings, so build per project; a real Kestrel host against PostgreSQL with the runtime login found what in-process tests without a database cannot.

## 2026-10-01 ARV-010b Server-side sessions, refresh rotation and revocation (PR #24)
- Summary: user_session and refresh_token (script 0004); a new session and refresh family at every sign-in, revoking a cookie sent along; a session check on every authenticated request in every host with a 4 second cache evicted on revocation; 12 hour absolute and role-based idle lifetimes; single-use refresh tokens with a grace successor for concurrent tabs and family revocation on reuse; the __Secure-ariva_rt cookie with an X-Ariva-Csrf and Origin check; administrator disable and enable; SignalR session filter and sweeper; /api and /hubs on the web host.
- Gates: backend PASS (410 unit, 43 integration with 1 skipped), e2e PASS, security PASS with two warnings (SEC-052 for login and refresh, PENDING approval).
- Security review: CWE-384 (new session at every sign-in, presented cookie revoked), CWE-613 (server-side expiry and revocation within 5 seconds), CWE-352 (SameSite=Strict, custom header and Origin allowlist on refresh), CWE-287 (refresh reuse detection).
- Learnings: PostgreSQL RETURNING returns the new row, so taking a value while clearing it needs the old row from a locked subquery; Playwright's request context sends Secure cookies to localhost, so E2E auth calls use fetch with explicit cookies; a 5 second cache measured about 5.15 seconds across hosts, so the cache is 4 seconds to keep the 5 second promise; login, refresh and password change share the per-address limit, which the in-process matrix run must lift.

## 2026-10-02 ARV-010c TOTP, recovery codes and break-glass access (PR #25)
- Summary: script 0005 (TOTP columns, break-glass flag with a unique partial index, recovery_code); RFC 6238 TOTP with a one-step window and a replay guard in one UPDATE on the user row; secrets Data Protection encrypted and shown once; enrolment confirmed by a first code; ten Crockford recovery codes, SHA-256 at rest, single use, regenerated with a code; wrong codes count towards the lockout; MFA at sign-in with amr pwd plus otp or rc; the break-glass account created and rotated only by the installer command, never locked out, a critical event at every sign-in.
- Gates: local harness PASS (every project built on its own, the TOTP and break-glass flows against PostgreSQL 16 through a real Kestrel host with the runtime login); GitHub CI not observed from this session (no push access after the context reset), so the PR checks are the outstanding gate.
- Security review: CWE-294 (replay guard in SQL), CWE-308 (TOTP for every human account), CWE-312 (secret encrypted, recovery codes hashed), CWE-307 (codes in the lockout), CWE-269 (break-glass outside the API).
- Learnings: the security scanner refuses stackalloc with a computed size, so TOTP uses fixed buffers; RFC 6238 vectors were checked against an independent Python implementation before the C# tests were written.

## 2026-10-02 ARV-010d Step-up MFA for critical actions
- Summary: [RequiresRecentMfa(15)] with on-demand RecentMfa policies reading amr and auth_time; the RFC 9470 401 (insufficient_user_authentication, max_age) with a mfa_required problem only when the second factor is the sole gap, 403 otherwise; POST /api/auth/step-up with a TOTP or recovery code that records the new auth_time on the session; security/critical-actions.json with an architecture test over every host; recovery code regeneration as the first critical action.
- Gates: local harness PASS (step-up flow through a real Kestrel host against PostgreSQL: stale token refused with the header, non-critical endpoint unaffected, step-up by recovery code in the same session, single use); GitHub CI not observed from this session.
- Security review: CWE-306 (critical functions need a recent second factor), CWE-287 (step-up shares the replay guard and lockout), CWE-204 (403 before step-up for callers without the permission, so the 401 never reveals an action).
- Learnings: AuthorizationHandlerContext.Fail empties FailedRequirements, so a handler that wants the result handler to see which requirement failed must leave it pending rather than fail it.

## 2026-10-02 ARV-011 User, role and audit administration
- Summary: api/v1/admin/users (search, view, create with a one-time temporary password, update, password and TOTP reset, grant and revoke), api/v1/admin/roles and the read-only api/v1/admin/audit-entries; ranked fixed roles with no self-change, nothing above the granter's rank and the last active administrator kept; create, resets, grants and revokes as critical actions; audit_entry written in the same transaction and append-only for the runtime login (script 0006); session revocation through one AccountSessions path.
- Gates: local harness PASS (34 checks through a real Kestrel host against PostgreSQL 16 with the runtime login, including UPDATE and DELETE on audit_entry refused with 42501, and the session and TOTP flows rerun after the refactor); GitHub CI not observed from this session.
- Security review: CWE-269 (rank check, no self-change, last administrator, step-up on every grant), CWE-863 (permission per action, break-glass invisible), CWE-306 (critical actions), CWE-89 (sort allowlist, parameterised filters), CWE-532 and CWE-312 (no credentials in audit summaries or logs), CWE-640 (temporary passwords random, shown once, forcing a change).
- Decisions: roles stay the four fixed codes (custom roles and a second approving administrator are Phase 1 candidates); accounts are disabled, never deleted; the last-administrator rule excludes the break-glass account.
- Learnings: fetching a collection together with Skip and Take pages the joined rows, so roles for a page of users come from a second query.

## 2026-10-02 ARV-012 Site scoping
- Summary: site, user_site and user.all_sites (script 0007); ISiteScope per request with the role cache and eviction; WithinSites for ISiteBound queries; [SiteScoped] answering 404 outside the caller's sites; an architecture test over action parameters, route templates and request model properties; api/v1/sites, api/v1/admin/sites and the critical PUT api/v1/admin/users/{id}/sites; E2E accounts bound to sites.
- Gates: local harness PASS (site listing and IDOR 404s, grant rules, narrowing ending sessions, site-limited administrators, break-glass bootstrap and a concurrent mutual revoke through a real Kestrel host against PostgreSQL); GitHub CI not observed from this session.
- Security review: an independent reviewer (separate agent) found one high (site access could not be granted outside vm-local), two medium (site-limited administrators acting on wider accounts; a last-administrator race) and three low issues; all fixed in fa7876b and re-verified. Final verdicts: ARV-010d PASS, ARV-011 PASS, ARV-012 PASS. CWE-863, CWE-269, CWE-362, CWE-204 reviewed.
- Deferred: binding integration clients to sites lands with the integration client entity (ARV-042); the reviewer's remaining low notes are closed (siteCodes in the architecture rule, administrators without sites reserved to all-sites callers, the break-glass incident path in wiki 04).
- Learnings: a seed that inserts the same natural key for two accounts in one session needs its own set, because the LINQ existence query does not see unflushed inserts; script 0007 was edited before it ever ran outside a throwaway database, which is the only time an edit is acceptable.

## 2026-10-02 ARV-013 Site topology entities
- Summary: Airport (IATA, ICAO, IANA time zone), Terminal (bound to a site), Level (floor number and extent in metres), Checkpoint (CheckIn, Security, Emigration, Immigration) and Desk (Counter, SecurityLane, Desk, EGate) as soft-deletable entities built through their parents, which enforce unique codes among live children, unique floor numbers, desk kinds by checkpoint kind and lane category rules by desk kind; the site code flows down from the terminal (ISiteBound).
- Gates: 246 unit tests executed offline (the TopologyTests suite plus the existing domain, persistence and security tests that need no host) with a functional xunit and FluentAssertions shim, because NuGet is unreachable from this session; GitHub CI not observed.
- Security review: CWE-501 (codes and kinds validated in the entity, site code never bound from a request, set from the terminal).

## 2026-10-02 ARV-014 Topology persistence and AdminArea APIs
- Summary: script 0008 (airport, terminal, level, checkpoint, desk with soft delete, partial unique indexes on live codes, site_code foreign keys, no DELETE for the runtime login); SvcTopology with site-scoped searches and lookups, entity invariants as validation, soft deletes refused while children live, audit entries per change and cached single-record reads under the topology tag; five AdminArea controllers with [Permission] per action and [SiteScoped]; 25 matrix rows; .http requests.
- Gates: local harness PASS (39 topology checks through a real Kestrel host against PostgreSQL, including cross-site 404s, SQL and markup payloads, cache eviction and the audit trail; every main-host permission matrix row, 265 cells, against the real host with real tokens); 246 unit tests executed offline; GitHub CI not observed.
- Security review: CWE-862 (permission per action, writes for administrators only), CWE-863 (site filter on every query and lookup, parents outside the caller's sites answer 404, airports deployment-wide), CWE-89 (parameterised LINQ, sort allowlist, page cap), CWE-501 (site code and parent fixed at creation, kinds parsed by name only).
- Learnings: NHibernate LINQ resolves a member accessed through an interface constraint (ISiteBound.SiteCode) to the mapped property, so one WithinSites helper serves every site-bound entity; session.Get ignores the soft delete filter, so lookups check IsDeleted themselves.

## 2026-10-02 ARV-015 Desk ranges and external desk code mapping
- Summary: Checkpoint.AddDeskRange (prefix plus padded numbers, at most 200, every code valid and free or nothing added) behind POST api/v1/admin/desks/range; DeskCodeMapping (script 0009) for AMAN desk and gate codes and AODB counter codes, unique per system and site and one per desk and system, fitted to the desk kind, kept in its site, re-pointable and soft deleted; api/v1/admin/desk-code-mappings; ISvcDeskCodeMappings.ResolveAsync for the feed consumers, cached under the topology tag.
- Gates: 267 unit tests executed offline; local harness PASS (ranges, overlap refusal leaving nothing behind, mapping uniqueness and kind rules, cross-site 404, resolver per site and cache eviction; the permission matrix, 295 cells, against the real host); GitHub CI not observed.
- Security review: CWE-120 (range size, prefix and code lengths bounded before anything is built), CWE-863 (mappings site-scoped, a code cannot move to another site).

## 2026-10-02 ARV-016 Zone profile aggregate
- Summary: ZoneProfile (Draft, Published, Retired) with Zone (Queue, Service, Staff, Overflow) and Line (Entry, Exit, Count, OverflowEntry); a pure geometry kernel (simple polygon, shoelace area, segment intersection, line on edge, millimetre rounding); invariants for polygons, names, links, lines on edges, one exit per queue and level bounds; Validate for publishing; immutable published versions; drafts copied from a version; a geometry hash with a documented canonical form (formulas F22). The prototype's snake queue, service area, overflow band and count line map to the glossary's Queue, Service and Overflow zones and Count lines.
- Gates: 331 unit tests executed offline, including 75 table-driven geometry and profile tests; the reference hash was recomputed independently in Python; GitHub CI not observed.
- Security review: CWE-501 (geometry validated in the aggregate, coordinates bounded by the level, vertex and zone counts capped; immutability enforced in the entity).
- Learnings: rounding can produce negative zero, which prints as -0.000 and would change the hash for the same geometry; adding 0.0 after rounding normalises it.


## 2026-10-02 ARV-018 Floor plan assets
- Summary: one floor plan per level at `api/v1/admin/levels/{levelId}/floor-plan` (upload, metadata, content, calibration, delete), PNG, JPEG or SVG up to 20 MB, typed by the bytes; SVG streamed through an SVG-namespace element and attribute allowlist with local-only references; files behind `IFileStorage` (local disk now, S3-compatible later) under generated keys; script 0010 with a partial unique index for one live plan; replacement under a level row lock with a flush before the insert; a level with a live plan cannot be deleted. Chart: `ariva-files` claim mounted at `/app/data/files` (ReadWriteOnce with Recreate in dev, demo and local; ReadWriteMany in production), api-main `/tmp` raised to 128Mi, api-main and web-api ingress body size 21m.
- Gates: 651 unit tests and 68 integration tests (PostgreSQL 16) offline; the real api-main host on Kestrel passed 21 floor plan checks (replace, sanitised SVG, 413 at 21 MB and 22 MB, traversal and right-to-left names, burst of six uploads giving 201 x3 and 429 x3) and the 320-cell permission matrix; E2E spec type-checked, not run (no E2E stack here); `helm template` not run (helm not reachable from this environment); GitHub CI not observed.
- Security review (independent reviewer): CWE-79 PASS, CWE-120 PASS, CWE-501 PASS after fixes. Fixed from the review: the SVG filter was a denylist (XHTML, MathML and CDATA passed; `url()` beyond the first and CSS escapes passed), now an allowlist; SVG element flood (925 MB allocated for 20 MB) now capped at 200,000 elements and depth 64 and streamed; concurrent uploads could exhaust memory and `/tmp`, now one at a time per host (`upload` concurrency policy, 429 beyond two waiting); PNG and JPEG pixel bombs refused above 16,384 a side or 128 megapixels, IHDR and start-of-frame required; the production chart would not render (ReadWriteOnce with an HPA of 2 to 4), now ReadWriteMany with the guard also reading `apiMainHpaMin`; the headers middleware kept any endpoint CSP, now only one that starts from `default-src 'none'` and denies framing; E2E called the wrong calibration route and the 413 check was optional; orphaned `.partial` files and plans on deleted levels; right-to-left override and split surrogates in display names.
- Also fixed: `TemporaryPasswords` could draw a password that spells the site code (about one draw in a thousand for AMM), which the password policy refuses and which made `TemporaryPasswords_Should_PassThePolicyAndNeverRepeat_When_Generated` flaky; it now redraws until the policy passes (ARV-011 follow-up).
- Learnings: NHibernate flushes inserts before updates, so a soft delete that frees a partial unique index must be flushed before the replacing insert. ASP.NET buffers multipart files over 64 KB to the temp directory before the action runs, so upload concurrency has to be limited in the rate limiter, not in the service.

## 2026-10-02 ARV-020 Messaging over Kafka with outbox and dead letters
- Summary: `KafkaTopics` (ADR-0019 names, dead-letter names, AMAN feed under Ariva's prefix) and `[KafkaTopic]` on events; `ISvcMessageBus` on the MassTransit 8 Kafka Rider (in-memory bus), keyed producers per event topic and per dead-letter topic (acks all, idempotence, 1 MB, no linger), plain JSON values with tracing headers; script 0011 with `outbox_message` and `processed_event`; the NHibernate outbox in the entity's transaction; a single-leader relay (advisory lock, `FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED`, seq order, a failed key held back until its retry, a broker failure ends the pass, unknown types wait without attempts); the inbox and unit of work filter; the dead-letter filter; topic provisioning in the background (Api.Main); one consumer group per service and purpose through `RegisterArivaServices(configuration, m => m.Consume<,>(topic, purpose))`; `PartitionedConsumer<T>` for Stream with manual commits after checkpoints; readiness on checks tagged `ready` (the MassTransit bus); the MassTransit meter in telemetry; SASL over TLS required in production with the CA and password mounted from optional secrets.
- Finding: MassTransit puts `UseMessageRetry` first in the message pipe whatever the configuration order (pipe probe), so the dead-letter filter sits inside the retry and acts on the final attempt only; ADR-0018 records it.
- Gates: 682 unit tests (including the in-memory pipe tests: retries then one dead letter, fresh unit of work per attempt, duplicates skipped, consumer timeouts dead-lettered, refused dead letters retried) and 74 integration tests on PostgreSQL 16 (outbox in the caller's transaction, order per key, broker failure, leader lock, unknown type, inbox) offline; a real host started with Kafka enabled and no broker stays up and serves; 320-cell permission matrix and floor plan checks still pass. Not run here: the four Testcontainers.Kafka tests (compiled against a stub; need Docker), the build against the pinned MassTransit 8.5.11 and Confluent.Kafka 2.15.1 (compiled offline against 8.4.0 and 2.8.0), `helm template`.
- Security review (independent reviewer): CWE-77 PASS, CWE-120 PASS, CWE-501 PASS after fixes (it was FAIL: production Kafka was plaintext without authentication). Also fixed from the review: dead letters over 1 MB wedged the partition (body now cut at 512 KB), `Ignore<OperationCanceledException>` let a consumer timeout skip retry and dead letter, the stream consumer auto-committed offsets past unpersisted state and could not dead-letter (no producer, only JSON errors caught, null headers), inbox kept shorter than topic retention (now 35 days), oversized events now fail their commit, unknown types no longer back off, the relay producer no longer lingers.
- Follow-ups: replay from a dead-letter topic as an audited admin command (ADR-0018, not in this story's criteria); Strimzi `KafkaTopic` resources for clusters where hosts may not create topics.

## 2026-10-02 ARV-017 Zone profile API with publish workflow
- Summary: `api/v1/admin/zone-profiles` (history of a site, a version with zones and lines, draft from the published version, rename, zones and lines on the draft, validation with the geometry hash, publish, discard); script 0012 (zone_profile, zone, line; one draft and one published version per site, unique versions, same-profile foreign keys, triggers that keep published and retired geometry fixed, SQLSTATE 23001); `ZoneProfilePublished` raised by the aggregate and written to the outbox (key site, topic `ariva.topology.zone-profile-activated.v1`); publishing is critical (`[RequiresRecentMfa]`, critical-actions.json) and bound to the reviewed geometry hash; row locks on the profile (edits, publish) and the site (draft, publish); audit per change, with zone and line details.
- Also fixed: NHibernate passed every string with a size of 1,000 and Npgsql silently cuts a longer value to the parameter size (verified), so audit summaries over 1,000 characters were truncated; `[MaxLength]` on a property now sets its size (polygons 4,000, audit summaries 2,000). The ARV-010d step-up E2E spec called critical routes with literal `{id:guid}` placeholders and used a supervisor who gets 403 on user routes; it now fills route values like the matrix and uses administrators.
- Gates: 684 unit tests and 79 integration tests on PostgreSQL 16 (workflow, events in the outbox, triggers, 200-point polygon stored whole, foreign ids, stale hash, same-profile keys); the real host passed 24 workflow checks (MFA 401, handler 403, two concurrent publishes give 200 and 409) and the 380-cell permission matrix; E2E spec type-checked, not run.
- PRD note: the criterion says publish without recent MFA returns 403; ADR-0026 and ARV-010d answer 401 with the RFC 9470 challenge, and the spec expects 401.
- Security review (independent reviewer): CWE-306 PASS, CWE-862 PASS, CWE-863 PASS. Fixed from the review: a publish racing an edit could publish geometry other than the hash (now locked before load, trigger reads with FOR SHARE), publish not bound to the reviewed content (geometry hash in the request), unbounded history (projection with SQL counts, newest 200), audit without geometry detail, retirement date rewritable and cross-profile zone links (trigger and composite keys), stale concurrent publish answering 400, raw exception messages (only domain rule messages are returned).
- Follow-ups: retire without a replacement and activation from a time (admin guide target); a floor plan overlay in the zone editor UI.

## 2026-10-02 ARV-019 Seed the DMO demo topology
- Summary: `DemoTopologySeed` creates the prototype's fictional Demo International Airport: site `DMO`, terminal `T1`, levels `ARR` and `DEP` (100 by 60 m), check-in `CI` with 48 counters (A01 to D12), security `SEC-N` and `SEC-S` with 5 lanes each, arrival immigration `IMM` (AR-01 to AR-22 by lane: crew, citizens, residents, visitors; e-gates AG-1 to AG-6) and departure immigration `EMI` (DP-01 to DP-22, DG-1 to DG-4), and zone profile v12 from the prototype's `floorplan.js` at 0.1 m per unit (16 queues, 7 overflow bands, 39 lines), published by `demo-seed` with `ZoneProfilePublished` in the outbox and a `ZoneProfile.Published` audit entry carrying the geometry hash. Idempotent: only missing records are created, no updates; a transaction advisory lock serialises pods and the site row lock serialises it with the zone profile API. Runs in the background in Api.Main with retries (5 s to 2 min, then gives up without stopping the host). On with `Seed:DemoTopology` (vm-local appsettings) or the Helm value `demoSeed` (k8s-demo); refused unless both `DOTNET_ENVIRONMENT` and `Application:Environment` are vm-local, k8s-dev or k8s-demo, and the chart refuses `demoSeed` for k8s-prd.
- Gates: 700 unit tests (profile validates with 23 zones and 39 lines, levels per zone, stable hash, environment allow-list including a prd host whose settings say vm-local, retry and give-up) and 82 integration tests on PostgreSQL 16 (two seeds at once then a third: one creates, the others create nothing, snapshot unchanged, counts, version 12, one outbox row, audit with hash, no users; an existing draft is left alone and v12 skipped; a T1 of another site stops the seed with nothing written) offline; the real api-main host with the seed on serves DMO with v12, 23 zones, 39 lines, 112 desks and 5 checkpoints, and an AMM supervisor gets 404; 380-cell matrix, floor plan and zone profile checks still pass. Not run here: `helm template` (helm not reachable; the new chart guard case is in chart-security.mjs), the four Testcontainers.Kafka tests.
- Security review (independent reviewer): CWE-269 FAIL then PASS after fixes. Fixed from the review: the production guard read only `Application:Environment`, which defaults to vm-local when a cluster's mounted settings file omits it (now both the host environment and the setting must be on the allow-list, plus a chart guard); the k8s-demo appsettings change never reached a cluster (mounted settings replace it; now a Helm value); a draft made through the API made the seed skip v12 forever without saying so, and inserting v12 next to it would break the one-draft index (now skipped with a logged reason); no site row lock and no publish audit with the hash; one failure at startup meant no demo data until restart (now retried in the background); a T1 or checkpoint of another site or kind was reused (now refused before writing); the system identity looked like a real user id (now `Guid.Empty`, like the development user seed).
- Learnings: NHibernate's insert action captures the entity's state at `Save`, not at flush, so an entity saved as a draft and published before commit is inserted as a draft and updated after; a partial unique index on drafts sees the intermediate state.

## 2026-10-02 ARV-021 Devices, calibrations and the canonical sensing model
- Summary: canonical sensing records in `Ariva.Core.Sensing` (`TrackPosition`, `LineCrossing`, `ZoneOccupancy`, `IntervalCount`, `DeviceStatus`) with `CanonicalEventRules` (bounds for times, coordinates, heights, track ids namespaced by device, names, counts, intervals, health); `Device` (family, model, transport, dialect, clock source, level, position, mounting height 2 to 20 m, orientation, coverage footprint from the vendor or the BOQ's assumed table and labelled, owning queue zone by name, credential prefix and hash) and `DeviceCalibration` (method, sample 50 to 5,000, counting accuracy, wait-time error, threshold 95 percent by default); lifecycle Commissioning, Online after a passed calibration for a published zone the footprint reaches, back to Commissioning when moved or when the model, transport or dialect changes, Degraded and Offline only for a commissioned device, Retired final; `DeviceRegistryChanged` on `ariva.device.registry-changed.v1` keyed by site; script 0013 (device, device_calibration, check constraints, immutable calibrations); `api/v1/admin/devices` (search, assumed footprint, view, register, details, placement, credential rotation, calibrations, retire, remove); 11 matrix rows; register, rotate and retire are critical actions.
- Decision: a device names its owning queue zone by the zone's name in the site's profiles, not a zone id: every profile version copies zones with new ids and keeps names, so the key of a device's events stays stable across versions (ADR-0019 keys sensor events by the owning zone). Recorded in wiki/11 and docs/architecture/sensor-adapters.md.
- Also fixed: every `GeneratedRegex` anchored with `$` in Core and Infra (topology codes, sites, desk code mappings, file keys, track ids) used `$`, which in .NET also matches before a final line break; they now end with `\z`. A level with a device that is not retired can no longer be deleted.
- Gates: 791 unit tests (lifecycle, footprint table, ring and overlap geometry, canonical bounds, credential format, hidden characters, the permission matrix in process) and 86 integration tests on PostgreSQL 16 (register with the credential stored only as prefix and hash and absent from audit and outbox, duplicate, out of reach, overflow-band coverage, failed then passed calibration, calibrations unchangeable and a forged pass refused by SQL, move back to commissioning, refused move changing nothing, rotation, retire revoking the hash, removal only before calibration, zone only in the draft, other sites, concurrent registration of one code) offline; the real api-main host passed 36 device checks and the 435-cell permission matrix; zone profile and floor plan checks still pass; E2E spec type-checked, not run.
- Security review (independent reviewer): CWE-287 PASS, CWE-501 PASS, CWE-863 PASS. Fixed from the review: a level with devices could be deleted (now refused, and views tolerate a missing level); a vendor footprint under 0.05 m rounded to 0 and failed the database check (now at least 0.1 m after rounding); codes with a trailing line break passed the `$` anchor; a passing calibration did not check that the footprint reaches the published zone (now checked under the site lock); free text could spoof fields in audit summaries (now escaped); retiring was not a critical action (now it is); a new model, transport or dialect kept a calibration measured with the old one (now back to commissioning); a credential prefix clash is now avoided by drawing again. Noted for ARV-022 and ARV-023: bound future timestamps against the device clock.
- Research (project note `claude/sensor-commissioning-2026-10.md`): vendors put the pushing credential on a parent, gateway or perception server that stands for many sensors (Xovis multi-sensor parents, SPIDER, LiDAR platforms); ARV-022 should authenticate the pushing endpoint and map its sensors. The 95 percent threshold matches Axis's floor but sits under the 98 to 99 percent 3D vendors claim; validation should report per-bucket absolute error over at least 100 passages, and re-validation should be scheduled and event-triggered rather than a fixed recalibration.

## 2026-10-02 ARV-022 Device authentication scheme
- Summary: a device scheme of its own (`Ariva.Device`) with `[DeviceAuthenticated]` (any device) and `[DeviceAuthenticated(ownZoneOnly: true)]` (the route's `zone` must be the device's own queue zone, else 403); the credential as `Authorization: Bearer ardk_...`, `X-Ariva-Device-Key`, or Basic with the device code as user name; the SHA-256 compared in constant time (a miss hashes against a placeholder); per-device source networks (CIDR, after trusted proxies) and an optional pinned client certificate (SHA-256), set with `PUT api/v1/admin/devices/{id}/access` (critical, script 0014); a `device` rate limit policy per credential; Ingest endpoints `GET api/v1/ingest/device` (who the device is, with the server clock) and `GET api/v1/ingest/zones/{zone}` (the published zones and lines of its zone); `ISvcDeviceGateway` with a memory-only, fail-safe-off lookup cached a minute under the `devices` tag, evicted after every registry change; the matrix gains an access type `device` (every user caller 401). Ingest raises its per-address limit to 20,000 a minute (gateways and NAT).
- Gates: 815 unit tests (in-process Ingest host: missing, wrong, unknown and malformed keys 401, user tokens 401, Bearer, header and Basic 200, Basic with another code 401, own zone 200 and another 403, networks with IPv4-mapped addresses, pinned and other certificates, forwarded certificates only from the trusted proxy, per-device limits that a known prefix cannot exhaust, device keys refused on user endpoints, redaction of device keys) and 87 integration tests on PostgreSQL 16 (lookup by prefix, eviction on access change, rotation and retirement, the zone geometry) offline; the real host with Main and Ingest controllers passed 16 device authentication checks against PostgreSQL (rotation and retirement take effect at once), 38 registry checks and the 440-cell matrix; E2E spec type-checked, not run.
- Security review (independent reviewer): CWE-287 PASS, CWE-306 PASS, CWE-863 PASS. Fixed from the review: the credential lookup inherited fail-safe (a stale record for up to an hour when the database is slow) and wrote misses and hashes to Redis (now memory only, no fail-safe, no soft timeout); the per-device rate limit was keyed by the public prefix, so anyone could exhaust a real sensor's window (now a hash of the whole credential); the forwarded client certificate was trusted from any caller (now only from a trusted proxy, read before the forwarded headers, and removed otherwise); device keys were not redacted in logs; leaving the certificate out of an access change dropped the pin (both fields are now required); device bearer keys made the JWT handler fail on every push (now skipped). Deployment: restrict `Security:ForwardedHeaders` for Ingest to the ingress and add a NetworkPolicy (wiki/04).
- Follow-ups: a gateway that pushes for several sensors (Xovis parents, perception servers) with ARV-023; bound future event times against the device clock (ARV-023).

## 2026-10-02 ARV-023 HTTPS push ingest with the Xovis dialect
- Summary: `POST api/v1/ingest/zones/{zone}/events` (canonical dialect, strict JSON) and `POST api/v1/ingest/zones/{zone}/xovis` (Xovis firmware 5 data push: `logics_data` to interval counts and occupancy, `live_data` to tracks placed on the floor with the device pose, `connection_test` accepted, legacy formats refused) for the device's own zone and dialect; JSON only (415), 256 KB with and without a declared length (413), 2,000 events (`Ingest:MaxEventsPerMessage`, at most 3,000); every event checked against the canonical bounds and the published lines and zones of the device's zone; the per-device clock offset (F19) as an EWMA of send against receive times with the 10-reading stability test (`ClockOffset`, Ok, Corrected or Unreliable), resends and readings beyond a day ignored; events beyond 500 ms ahead or 10 minutes late flagged `Skewed`, beyond 5 minutes ahead or the 3-day retention refused; track ids namespaced by device; batches (`TrackSampleBatch`, `VendorLineCrossingBatch`, `ZoneOccupancyBatch`, `IntervalCountBatch`) keyed by `<site>/<queue zone>` with ids from a hash of the body, health reports keyed by device id at most every 10 seconds; 202 with the counts, 400, 503 only for broker failures. Two topics added: `ariva.device.zone-occupancy.v1` and `ariva.device.interval-count.v1`.
- Decisions: the PRD named one topic, `ariva.sensing.events.v1`; the topics follow ADR-0019 and the overview table, plus two for counting and occupancy devices (sensor-adapters.md). `DeviceRegistryChanged` is now keyed by device id, as the overview table says for its compacted topic (it was keyed by site in ARV-021, which compaction would have collapsed to one device per site).
- Vendor research (sources in docs/architecture/sensor-adapters.md): the logics push is verified against a published firmware 5.8.2 capture and the V5 API; the live push envelope from parser code; metres and the sensor frame for live positions are inferred and listed, with eight other points, to confirm with Xovis before certification. Conformance samples are shaped from those sources with our own values, with expected canonical events (Ariva.UnitTests/Sensing/Samples).
- Gates: 863 unit tests (conformance samples, malformed, legacy, oversized, deep and hostile payloads, strict canonical parsing, F19 cases, correction and skew, extreme times, forged and resent clock readings, names, dialects, ids for resends and counter restarts, health throttling, Kafka size of the largest batch, the Ingest host end to end: 202, 401, 403, 400, 413 with and without a length, 415, 503) and 87 integration tests offline; the real host with Main and Ingest controllers against PostgreSQL passed 17 push checks (registered canonical and Xovis devices, published zone, a 2 s clock offset estimated) plus device authentication, registry, matrix, zone profile, floor plan and demo seed checks.
- Security review (independent reviewer): CWE-120 PASS, CWE-501 PASS after fixes. Fixed from the review: batch ids from the vendor package id would drop new data after a counter restart (now a body hash, which also makes resends after a partial publish duplicates); one event at year 1 or 9999 with a corrected clock overflowed the correction and became an endless 503 (rejected events are no longer corrected, corrections stay in range, and only broker failures are 503); the clock estimate took readings of any size and stale resends (now ignored, and corrections use the stable mean); canonical errors echoed unknown member names and notes quoted raw vendor text (now known path segments only and a safe quoting); the event limit could exceed Kafka's 1 MB (capped at 3,000, tested); late flags covered tracks only (now every kind); status times were unchecked; slow bodies could hold connections (1 KB a second minimum); counts summed in int; an envelope with both logics and live data lost the live part.
- Follow-ups: recorded payloads from the pilot sensors before certification; commissioning data shares the topics, marked `Commissioned` false; gateways that push for several sensors.

## 2026-10-02 ARV-024 Declarative mapper and MQTT transport
- Summary: the `Declarative` dialect reads a vendor's JSON through a mapping shipped with Ariva (Ariva.Infra/Sensing/Mappings, embedded and checked at start-up, listed at `GET api/v1/admin/devices/mappings`); a device names it (`mappingName`, script 0015 with a check that ties it to the dialect); `POST api/v1/ingest/zones/{zone}/declarative`. Paths are a closed JSONPath subset (`$`, `^`, `@` roots, names, quoted names, indexes, one wildcard where a section enumerates; no filters, recursive descent, slices, unions, functions or expressions); sections for tracks, crossings, occupancy and intervals with equality filters, time units (RFC 3339, s, ms, us, ns, or receipt time without clock correction), a coordinate scale and floor or device frame. `ouster-detect-v1` maps Ouster Detect `object_list` (PERSON tracks in the device frame, microsecond times) and `occupations` (zone counts at receipt). The MQTT transport: an MQTTnet 5.2.0.1603 broker on its own Kestrel listener (8883, TLS, off by default), device code and credential checked like an HTTPS push, only for devices on the `Mqtt` transport, client id the device code, topic `ariva/v1/devices/<code>/<dialect>` only, no subscriptions, no retained or will messages, the same ingest and a per-device rate limit, access rechecked on every message; Helm `mqtt.*` with a TLS secret guard and a Service that keeps client addresses. HTTPS push now refuses devices registered on another transport (403).
- Decisions: mappings are code-reviewed files, not data editable at run time (CWE-94, CWE-501); JSON only, XPath is not implemented. The PRD asked for MQTTnet "at a version confirmed by the nuget MCP"; the NuGet MCP is not available here, so 5.2.0.1603 was confirmed from nuget.org's flat container index and built from the v5.2.0 source for the offline tests; CI restores the packages. The broker is embedded in Ariva.Api.Ingest (wiki/05 had it To confirm). The device-credential checks moved to `DeviceCredentialCheck`, shared by HTTPS and MQTT.
- Vendor research: Ouster Detect field names and units from Ouster's documentation (connecting to output, appendix), with key spelling to confirm on a capture; Outsight (binary OSEF) and Seoul Robotics SENSR (protobuf) cannot be mapped declaratively and need coded adapters; Blickfeld Percept's JSON nesting is not documented precisely enough.
- Gates: 985 unit tests (32 hostile paths refused, path evaluation, 22 broken mapping documents, the catalog, Ouster conformance samples with expected events, a mapping with every section, mistyped and missing values without payload echo, event and work limits, receipt times not corrected, the declarative and transport checks on the Ingest host, the packet limit across any chunking, the topic ACL, settings, the broker logic, and MQTT end to end over TLS on loopback: publish, PayloadFormatInvalid, refused subscription, foreign topic closes, MQTT 3.1.1, wrong credential, wrong client id, clear text refused, an oversize packet, a will, silent connections closed and per-address slots returned) and 88 integration tests on PostgreSQL 16 (mapping names, script 0015's check) plus the 4 Kafka tests that need Docker; the real host against PostgreSQL passed 11 MQTT checks (an Ouster occupations message over TLS into a zone batch, an HTTPS device refused, rotation closing a live connection, a foreign topic closing it), the 445-cell matrix and the device, authentication, push, zone profile, floor plan and demo seed checks; docs gate and security scan pass; E2E spec type-checked, not run; helm rendering is CI-only.
- Security review (independent reviewer): CWE-94 PASS, CWE-501 PASS; overall FAIL on the first pass, PASS after fixes, reproduced in a scratch harness. Fixed from the review: connections were never closed after MQTTnet finished behind TLS, so slots and memory leaked until restart (the middleware now stops its pump and returns; a silent connection is closed after the connect timeout); up to 520 KB could be buffered per connection before authentication (now the first packet must be a CONNECT of at most 8 KB and every packet stays under 8 KB until the broker accepts it, one packet buffered at most, and at most 50 connections per address); a will message would have been ingested after an unclean disconnect (refused); the per-message recheck ignored narrowed networks and a new certificate pin (now applied); an MQTT device could also push over HTTPS with a second rate limit (refused), and group fields were re-read per item (now once per group); MQTT 3.1.1 with an empty client id is refused before it is logged as connected. Accepted: a session takeover with CleanSession false keeps the old session identity, which fails closed after a rotation. Operational: a distributed client can hold slots for up to the connect timeout; monitor open connections, and raise the per-address cap where many sensors share one NAT address.
- Follow-ups: a recorded Ouster capture before certification; whether Xovis MQTT carries the push JSON; MQTT certificate rotation needs a rollout; open-connection metrics with ARV-025.

## 2026-10-02 ARV-025 Device heartbeat and degraded zones
- Summary: Main consumes `ariva.device.health.v1` (group `ariva-main.device-health`) into `device_heartbeat` (last seen, online flag, frame rate, temperature, Ariva's clock estimate; newer reports only; reports out of the canonical bounds, with an undefined clock state or naming another site dropped). A heartbeat monitor sweeps every `Devices:Health:SweepSeconds` (15) on one replica (advisory lock): a commissioned device not heard from for `Devices:Health:HeartbeatTimeoutSeconds` (180) goes Offline; a report brings it back Online, a report with `online` false makes it Degraded, a report older than the timeout changes no state; Commissioning and Retired devices never change with health. `ZoneHealth` (script 0016) per queue zone: Healthy, Degraded while any commissioned device is offline or degraded, Unmonitored without one, re-assessed after every device change and by every sweep. Events: `DeviceRegistryChanged` (HealthOffline, HealthOnline, HealthDegraded) and the new `ZoneHealthChanged` on `ariva.device.zone-health.v1` (keyed by zone, compacted), through the outbox. API: `GET api/v1/admin/devices/health[?siteCode=]` (SearchDevice) and `GET api/v1/admin/devices/{id}/health` (ViewDevice), site-scoped. Metrics (meter `Ariva.Devices`): heartbeats lost, recoveries, zones degraded, and offline and degraded devices and degraded zones at the last sweep.
- Decisions: the overview's DeviceHeartbeatLost and DeviceRecovered are the registry event's HealthOffline and HealthOnline changes (one compacted topic per device), and zone health has a topic of its own; the default timeout is 180 seconds so that vendors pushing every minute stay online, and every device must push or report at least every half of it (wiki/04, wiki/17). A row lock per device serialises reports, the sweep and admin changes; the sweep skips devices a report holds and re-reads their heartbeat after locking; zones are assessed under a per-zone advisory lock. Playwright functional tests: no device screen exists yet, so the story's end-to-end tests are API tests (tests/api/device-health.spec.ts).
- Gates: 1001 unit tests (zone states and events, settings, topic, metrics; the matrix in process), 93 integration tests on PostgreSQL 16 including 5 for health (timeout to Offline, zone Degraded and back, out-of-order, stale and malformed reports, site scoping, one sweep at a time, Commissioning and Retired untouched, outbox events) plus the 4 Kafka tests that need Docker; the real host against PostgreSQL passed 13 health checks with a 30-second timeout (a status push recorded, Online, Offline after silence, zone Degraded, recovery), the 455-cell matrix, and the device, authentication, push, MQTT, zone profile, floor plan and demo seed checks; E2E spec type-checked, not run; docs gate and security scan pass. One pre-existing test (`AccessTokenTests.Load_Should_CreateOneSharedDevelopmentKey_When_HostsStartTogether`) failed once in a full run and passed three times alone: a timing flake to watch.
- Security review (independent reviewer): CWE-862 PASS; overall FAIL on the first pass, PASS after fixes. Fixed from the review: the sweep compared zones against copies loaded before the zone lock, so a report committing in between could leave a zone published Healthy with a device offline until the next sweep (now plain rows, and the zone is read after its lock); an undefined clock state from Kafka would fault the consumer into dead letters (dropped); a report naming another site was applied (dropped); the overview read up to 10,000 heartbeats across sites and could drop offline devices (now per listed site, offline first, with a truncation flag); recovery counted Offline to Degraded; advisory lock keys shared one hash space (now class 25). Info: one heartbeat query per site; no interleaving test for the zone fix.
- Follow-ups: Stream flags bins Degraded from `ariva.device.zone-health.v1`; a device screen with health; the heartbeat table could become the `device_health` hypertable for outage evidence (overview section 7).

## 2026-10-02 ARV-026 Raw sensing event archive
- Summary: script 0017 (the PRD's "0002" predates the numbering) creates `sensing_event` (one row per accepted canonical event: zone, device, batch and position, flags, receipt time, the fields of its kind), `sensing_batch` (each archived batch once) and `sensing_day_key`; the runtime role cannot update, delete or truncate the archive. TimescaleDB is required: both tables become hypertables (1-day chunks), `sensing_event` compressed after one day, both dropped after 90 days (To confirm per site); without TimescaleDB the migration fails unless the database sets `ariva.allow_plain_postgres` (local tests). `SensingArchive` writes batches by binary COPY (chunks of 20,000) under batch-id advisory locks with a 3-day duplicate window, and reads one zone over at most 31 days in time order, a day per statement; the Stream host archives the four sensing topics (`ariva-stream.sensing-archive-*`).
- Decisions: track ids are archived as pseudonyms of their UTC day (HMAC-SHA256 with a random daily key destroyed after three days), which keeps D4 (track ids never persist past the operating day) while tracks stay usable within the day for disputes; the operating day is the UTC day until the site's local day is decided (To confirm). Batches and events are checked again after Kafka and bad ones skipped and counted rather than failing their batch. Events stamped at receipt (ARV-024) now include the receipt time in their batch id, since the same body later is a new reading (found in this review). The consumer writes one Kafka message per transaction (MassTransit batch consumers do not fit the inbox filter); throughput is for the ARV-071 load test.
- Gates: 1003 unit tests and 101 integration tests on PostgreSQL 16 (8 for the archive: every kind written and read back, each batch once including two concurrent writers, a 24,000-event write over two COPYs, ranges and injection-shaped input refused or inert, daily pseudonyms and key destruction, bad events and batches skipped, the runtime role refused UPDATE, DELETE and TRUNCATE, TimescaleDB required) plus the 4 Kafka tests that need Docker; the TimescaleDB branch (hypertables, policies, a chunk DELETE refused) runs only in CI on the TimescaleDB image; the real-host matrix, push and health checks pass; docs gate and scan pass.
- Security review (independent reviewer): CWE-89 PASS; overall FAIL, then PASS after two rounds. Fixed: track ids kept 90 days against D4 (now daily pseudonyms); a silent fallback to no retention without TimescaleDB (now an error); a database failover dead-lettering a stretch of evidence (outages are waited out for about a minute per attempt, within Kafka's poll interval); one bad event dead-lettering its batch, unchecked flags, more than 3,000 events and malformed codes (skipped and counted); unbounded replay statements (a day each); lock order on hash keys; a key cached from a rolled-back write splitting a day's pseudonyms (keys cached only after commit). Accepted: the runtime role can delete day keys (needed to destroy them); keys live on in backups for the backup period (To confirm with the backup policy); how the MassTransit rider polls during a long consume is to verify in CI.
- Follow-ups: the CI TimescaleDB run; a test that forces a failure after the day-key insert; the Stream engine (ARV-034) reads replays from the archive.

## 2026-10-02 ARV-027 Port the reference scenario engine
- Summary: the prototype's seeded day (`docs/design/prototype/app/assets/sim.js`) is ported to C# in `Ariva.Simulation.Api/Scenarios/Engine`: seeded randomness (mulberry32, mix32, h3, FNV-1a), the 16 queues, 59 sensors and scripted evening, the schedule, the day-ahead roster, server pauses and unknown states, the per-minute backlog recursion with the network inflows, cumulative curves and realised waits, live state and nowcast (a band while a zone is degraded), 15-minute bins and P90s, the expected and day-ahead runs, the Monte Carlo forecast, staffing recommendations, demand and hall curves, and alert rules R-001 to R-005. Default seed 9303 (`Simulation:Seed`). The simulator runs the reference day at start and serves it under `api/v1/simulation/scenario`: the summary, every queue or one queue at a clock minute, the sensors and the alerts (read scope), and a re-run with another seed (control scope, six a minute per key, one at a time).
- Decisions: parity is bit for bit, not approximate: `scripts/simulation/reference-golden.mjs` runs sim.js for six cases (the reference day with and without the morning's allocations, seeds 1, 2026 and 4294967295, and a day with ad-hoc flights, an override and an accepted recommendation) and writes SHA-256 fingerprints of 15 output families; `ScenarioParityTests` matches all 90, and `verify.mjs unit` fails when the golden file no longer matches sim.js. `Math.pow` differs in the last bit between V8 and the platform's libm for some inputs (found by the parity test), so the check-in show-up curve is stored as the prototype's float64 bits (recomputed in a test within a few ULP); everything else is integer or IEEE arithmetic in the same order, with stable sorts. The simulator authenticates with operator keys, not Ariva user tokens (it shares nothing with Ariva but Ariva.Business.Contracts): `Simulation:Control:Keys` holds names, SHA-256 digests and scopes in the simulation appsettings secret; with no key nothing authenticates; an invalid key list or seed stops the host. The permission matrix gains an access kind `simulation` (every Ariva user gets 401).
- Gates: 1048 unit tests (90 parity fingerprints, determinism, the three scripted events: Visitors nowcast 16.3 minutes at 18:05 and never above 15 before, S-17 offline 18:20 to 18:30 with the zone degraded and a band, Handler B's bins 19:00, 19:15 and 19:30 above 15 with R-004 raised at 19:10; 29 endpoint tests for keys, scopes, validation, rate limit and startup; the matrix in process) on the offline harness; the real Kestrel host answered 401 without a key, 200 with one, re-ran seed 7 and logged the operator name; docs gate and security scan pass. `AccessTokenTests.Load_Should_CreateOneSharedDevelopmentKey_When_HostsStartTogether` failed again in the full run (a real race in the development key, fixed in the next commit).
- Security review (independent reviewer): CWE-306 PASS, overall PASS on the first pass. Fixed anyway from its Low and Info findings: a body without `seed` re-ran the day with seed 0 (now 400), an invalid `Simulation:Seed` only failed on the first request (the day is built at start), re-runs could run in parallel (one at a time), binding errors named internal types (hidden), duplicate key digests were accepted (refused). Accepted: no throttle on failed keys (keys are at least 32 random characters; keep the simulator off public ingress where possible).
- Follow-ups: the sensor, AODB and AMAN emulators (ARV-028 onward) replay this day; a scenario clock that maps wall time to demo minutes.

## 2026-10-02 ARV-010a follow-up: development signing key race
- Summary: hosts starting together on a developer machine could each write a development signing key: `File.Move` without overwrite is not atomic on every file system (it checks, then renames), so a host could read the public half from one key and the private half from another and refuse to start ("The signing key's public key is not among Auth:Tokens:PublicKeyPaths"). This was the `AccessTokenTests.Load_Should_CreateOneSharedDevelopmentKey_When_HostsStartTogether` flake seen in full runs since ARV-025. The key file is now created exclusively (`CreateNew`, unshared while written), other hosts read it once complete (retrying for up to five seconds), and both halves come from one read.
- Gates: a scratch stress run of eight concurrent loads, 300 rounds: 30 bad rounds before, 0 after; `AccessTokenTests` pass, including a new test for an incomplete key file. Development keys only (vm-local); clusters mount their keys from secrets.
- Security review (independent reviewer): PASS (CWE-362, CWE-276 and CWE-732, CWE-321 and CWE-798). Fixed from its Low finding: a host that fails while writing the key now deletes the partial file, and an incomplete key file fails with a message naming the file and saying to delete it. Info, not changed: an existing key file is used whatever its mode; `UseDevelopmentKeys` has no environment guard in code (only vm-local configuration sets it, and `PublicKeyPaths` in the cluster files turns it off).

## 2026-10-02 ARV-028 Sensor emulator
- Summary: `Ariva.Simulation.Api/Emulators/Sensors` plays the reference day to Ingest as device traffic over HTTPS push, authenticated with the credentials of devices registered in Ariva (from the simulation appsettings secret or `PUT api/v1/simulation/sensors/devices`; held in memory, never returned or logged). Each scenario sensor speaks the canonical or the Xovis dialect: the first sensor of a queue zone sends per-passenger crossings of the zone's entry and exit lines (one track id per passenger, FIFO) and its occupancy, or Xovis interval counts and a balance logic; the first sensor of an overflow band its occupancy beyond the snake capacity; the others a sign of life; S-17 nothing from 18:20 to 18:30. A demo clock runs at 0.5 to 60 demo minutes per wall minute and pushes each completed minute in order; controls (control scope): start (minute, speed, stop minute), pause, speed, jump, devices, and the seed through the scenario; the status (read scope) shows per device the pushes, the events expected and the events Ingest accepted. vm-local, k8s-dev and k8s-demo point the emulator at Ingest (in-cluster `http://api-ingest-service`).
- Decisions: passengers are the whole people of the fluid model, so occupancy is always entries minus exits and every exit is a passenger who entered; demo times map onto wall times so Ingest's clock checks see live traffic (at high speed a silent sensor is back before the heartbeat timeout, so degraded zones show only near speed 1). The PRD's "generic" dialect is the canonical one. Plain HTTP to Ingest needs `AllowInsecureTransport` and is accepted only for loopback or an in-cluster service name. The E2E run checks Ingest's 202 counts (Ingest answers 202 only after writing to Kafka) rather than reading Kafka itself, since the E2E stack has no Stream host or Kafka client.
- Gates: 1212 unit tests in the full run (every push of all 59 sensors in both dialects over the evening peak mapped through Ingest's own canonical and Xovis mappers, canonical rules and the DMO profile's names; passenger conservation all day for every queue; S-17's silence; the clock, pause, speed, jump, stop minute and failures with a fake Ingest; 27 endpoint tests for scopes, validation, credential handling and startup validation; the matrix in process), plus the review's tests; the real Main and Ingest host against PostgreSQL with the simulator as a separate process passed 20 checks for a 10-minute run at speed 60 (5 DMO devices registered, 148, 5, 20, 30 and 30 events accepted exactly as expected, 128 crossings, 40 occupancy and 40 intervals in the sink, track ids namespaced, no credential in the simulator's log); E2E `sensor-emulator.spec.ts` type-checked, not run; docs gate and scan pass.
- Security review (independent reviewer): CWE-287 PASS, overall PASS on the first pass. Fixed from its Low findings: the push client used an environment proxy, which would have seen credentials on plain-HTTP in-cluster pushes (proxy off); a new speed given to a running start was applied to the elapsed time and could jump hundreds of minutes (re-anchored first); any http host passed with the flag (loopback or in-cluster names only, and the E2E sets the flag only for a loopback Ingest); four log lines per push (client logging removed); a NaN MaxSpeed passed validation. Also from its Info notes: the E2E retires its devices and clears the emulator afterwards, and Ingest's answers are read up to 64 KB. Accepted: counters can be slightly off when a start resets them during a push; package ids restart when devices are replaced.
- Follow-ups: tracks (positions) for T2 devices; the AODB, AMAN and immigration emulators (ARV-029) on the same clock.

## 2026-10-02 ARV-029 deferred
- Decision: ARV-029 (AODB, AMAN and immigration emulators) is moved after ARV-050. Its criteria need Ariva's Integration API with TOTP clients (ARV-042), the immigration endpoints (ARV-048) and the outbound AMAN connector with `TotpClientCredentials` (ARV-050), none of which exist yet, so an emulator built now could only be tested against itself. Its PRD dependencies list only ARV-027 and ARV-020; the order otherwise stays by priority.

## 2026-10-02 ARV-030 Pure queue state engine
- Summary: `Ariva.Core/Queueing`: `QueueStateEngine`, one per queue zone, takes crossings, interval counts, occupancy readings and track sightings (`QueueInput`) with the zone's lines and zones from the published profile (`QueueZoneGeometry`), and on each `Advance(reference time)` returns a `QueueStep`: the counted entries and exits, realised waits (Track, Fifo or Cumulative, with a Degraded flag), entrants resolved without a wait (Abandoned, Fragmented, Censored, Reanchored), the queue length (fresh occupancy readings of the queue and its overflow bands, else the people held) and the rejections (late with the earliest late time, future, unknown geometry, duplicates, reverse, unmatched exits, negative waits, forced advances). Rules and defaults in formulas F5 and F6 (implementation notes added).
- Decisions: late events are counted and not applied, so a processed minute never changes under the engine (ARV-031 recomputes as a revision); anonymous crossings of an overflow band's entry line are not entries (they would double count); a tracked exit never pairs with a person the same device tracks (track keys are namespaced by device), which keeps waits right when the engine starts mid-flow; re-anchoring needs every zone of the queue to read zero.
- Security review (independent reviewer): CWE-120 FAIL on the first pass (unbounded work and output from valid but hostile device input), then fixed: interval counts were inserted with a walk over everyone held and abandons scanned backwards (quadratic: 13 s for one interval, hours for one push), now appended in order and taken from an anonymous list, at most 5,000 people per interval; per-person movement records and unbounded per-step lists could exhaust memory, now movements are counted per minute and a step holds at most 100,000 records (it ends early, nothing lost); a full buffer moved the watermark to the oldest buffered event, even into the future, so one device could make every other device's events late, now only events already due are processed to make room, new ones are refused, and events ahead of the clock may fill a tenth of the buffer; hand-over expiry scanned every track per event (now a heap of deadlines); a tracked exit with an unknown key scanned and allocated per held person (now one look per device group, devices per zone capped at 64); counters are 64-bit; occupancy and interval values are checked in the engine; the settings' upper bounds are tighter; a censor time is never before the entry. Second pass FAIL on one new High: when the entrant bound censored the last person of a device's group, the new entrant went into a removed list and a later exit threw, wedging the partition (CWE-248); fixed by making room before the group lookup, with a regression test and a fuzz test that checks conservation; also intervals no longer spread behind the watermark after a quiet period. Third pass: CWE-120 PASS, overall PASS (400-seed invariant fuzz, hostile loads of ten million resolutions in about 5 s with bounded memory). Open Low: one device flooding more than about 3,300 events a second (buffer over lateness) gets others' events refused as BufferFull; the Stream host should give devices quotas and thin position samples to about one per track per second (ARV-031, ARV-034).
- Gates: 47 engine tests and 5 fuzz seeds (F5 and F6 cases, overtaking, overflow, cumulative curves, spreads clamped after processed minutes, abandonment, censoring at T_censor, fragmentation, re-anchoring and the residual flag, the queue length, out-of-order within the lateness, late and future events, Degraded inputs, the watermark never going back, duplicates, geometry, the three bounds, settings, a purity check of the source, a bounded day) and 3 against the reference evening through the emulator and Ingest's mappers (one tracked wait per passenger, nothing lost, cumulative-curve waits from Xovis counts within a minute of the tracked ones, the length following the readings).

## 2026-10-02 ARV-031 Realised wait attribution and bins
- Summary: `Ariva.Core/Queueing`: `WaitStatistics` (F7 weighted nearest rank, mean, share within target, sparse 30-second histograms that merge exactly) and `BinAccumulator`, which takes the queue engine's steps and publishes minute and bin results (`MinuteResult`, `BinResult`): entries, exits, waits with mean, P50, P90, P95, share and histogram, abandoned, fragmented, censored, re-anchored and rejected people, open people, status, quality, revision, zone profile version and late events. Rules as F6, F7 and F11 (implementation notes added), finals never change in place: late events (now reported by the engine per minute) produce `RecomputationRequest`s, and `BinRevisions.Next` gives revision r + 1 to the final bins a recomputation changes, with its reason. The engine now reports a pair whose exit precedes its entry as a `Rejected` resolution, so every person who entered is accounted for in a bin.
- Decisions: the engine's watermark already includes the lateness allowance, so a bin is final once that watermark passes its end and nobody who entered in it is unresolved; every bin the watermark passes is published (empty ones final with count 0); bins align to the site's day start; percentiles from the histogram (beyond 50,000 waits in a bin) and bins forced final by the open-bin bound are Degraded. Storage, the 15-minute continuous aggregate and the Stream wiring are ARV-034.
- Gates: 20 accumulator tests (the F6 cases: 18:14:30 to 18:40:00 in bin 18:00 and not final before; a fragmented track not holding its bin; empty bins final; lateness; censored above 5 percent; worst quality wins with outages Unknown; day-start alignment; the F7 cases: P90 9, median 5, mean 5.5 for 1 to 10 minutes, the weighted P90 of 20 and share 0.833, the minimum; minute aggregates; histogram merging and fallback; late events after and before finality; revisions only for changed finals; the open-bin bound; settings) and 2 against the reference day through the emulator, Ingest's mapper and the engine: island C breaches in exactly the 19:00, 19:15 and 19:30 bins, every bin of the day becomes final once, and the Visitors bins' mean waits are within a minute of the scenario's own fluid waits.
- Security review (independent reviewer, baseline): FAIL on the first pass, fixed: late events beyond 10,000 distinct minutes were folded into the earliest (the right bins were never recomputed, at 28 us per event), now the engine reports late minutes only within a 3-day horizon (older ones counted as beyond it), so the minutes held are bounded by the horizon; recomputation requests were merged into one span from the oldest late minute (a 26-year replay from one event), now one request per run of contiguous bins; 32-bit counters could wrap under interval floods and finalise a bin with people unresolved, now 64-bit throughout (histograms included); exact waits were bounded per minute rather than per bin and re-sorted three times on every update, now one budget per bin with minutes included, percentiles from one sort, and provisional percentiles from the histogram; a final bin could be reopened when no final bins were remembered (now a high-water mark), a day start in seconds split entries from their waits (now whole minutes), a watermark jump beyond the open-bin bound skipped bins silently (now reported as a gap, marks bounded at 1,000), the weighted percentile used an absolute tolerance (now relative), an outage marked after a bin was final is returned as a recomputation, and histogram reading validates its input. Re-review: PASS (400-seed conservation fuzz with every final bin balanced and its P90 equal to the brute-force nearest rank, the probes of the first pass rerun). Two Info edges at the ends of the calendar fixed; open for ARV-034: deduplicate or rate-limit recomputation requests per zone, since a device can still ask for one per bin of the 3-day horizon.
- Gates (after the fixes): 24 accumulator tests and 2 against the reference day; the engine's 60 tests; the full unit suite.

## 2026-10-02 ARV-032 Nowcast and throughput
- Summary: `Ariva.Core/Queueing/Nowcast.cs`: `Nowcast.Compute` (F8: (Q + 1) / mu, mu = beta x desks / cycle time + (1 - beta) x exits in the window / m, fast-track share, e-gate rejects; no service with a reason when nothing is open, mu is below 0.01 a minute, or the queue length or throughput data is missing; the exit-only estimate flagged Degraded), `NowcastDisplays.Next` (passenger-screen display: neutral message for stale data or no service, the degraded band at least 10 minutes wide, "under 5 min", 5-minute bands with hysteresis against W_ref), `Nowcast.EgateRate` (F12) and `ExitRate` (exits over the last m whole minutes from the engine's movements). Implementation note in F8.
- Gates (first pass): 19 tests (the F8 cases: 7.5 minutes and band 5 to 10, the blend 3.8 and 7.89, the fast track 15, no service, hysteresis 12, 15.5, 17.2, degraded bands 5 to 15 and 0 to 10, precedence; every no-service reason; e-gates; settings; the exit window) and one against the reference evening: with the emulator, Ingest's mapper, the queue engine and the exit term, the Visitors nowcast stays within 2 minutes of the scenario's around 18:05 and passes 15 minutes there.
- Security review (independent reviewer, baseline): FAIL on the first pass, fixed: a cycle time near zero gave an infinite throughput and NaN minutes that broke JSON serialisation (Medium), now cycles under 0.05 minutes count as unknown, throughput above 1,000 a minute and any non-finite wait give `Implausible`; (Q + 1) overflowed at int.MaxValue (now computed in double); a wait in the billions overflowed the band's int cast (now "over 120 min" at the ceiling, degraded or not, and the degraded band clamped with its 10-minute width); the band flapped at the under-5 edge and in degraded mode (hysteresis extended, Proposed); an unknown fast-track share or reject rate was taken as optimistic (now flags Degraded); a gap in exits read as zero exits (now `ExitWindow` reports observed minutes and `Complete`); exit counts could wrap (now saturating). Re-review: PASS (2,000,000-input fuzz through compute, display and JSON with no failure; 200,000 display walks all settling); its three Lows (the degraded band excluding a wait beyond the ceiling, no upper bound on the exit term, `EgateRate` with a zero minimum cycle) fixed as well. For ARV-033's caller: pass null exits when the window is not `Complete`, and set Degraded from the degraded-exit share.
- Gates (after the fixes): 61 nowcast tests; the full unit suite (1,353); security scan 0 errors; docs.

## 2026-10-02 ARV-033 Desk state engine
- Summary: `Ariva.Core/Desks`: `DeskRule` (F10 precedence and staleness at a moment, with `NextChange` for the moments a threshold passes), `DeskStateEngine` (many desks, signals reordered within the lateness allowance, exact time per state, `DeskTransition` and per-desk `DeskMinute` aggregates without identities, late and superseded signals, bounds on desks, buffered signals and records per step), `LaneDesks` (n_open and Degraded for the nowcast) and `LaneCycle` (transaction-weighted lane cycle time). Implementation note in F10 with the Proposed choices beyond the table.
- Gotchas: signals of the same moment are applied together before the state is read, or a heartbeat followed by a login at the same instant shows a zero-length Closed; a signal exactly at the watermark is not late.
- Gates: 17 rule tests (the six F10 cases, staleness and the AMAN-down fallback, sensor-only serving, present not processing, break, transactions without a staff zone, transactions outranking an empty staff zone, a lost transaction end, the next threshold, settings) and 18 engine tests (exact minutes around a transaction, T1 and T2 transitions at the exact moment, staleness and recovery, reordering, a late logout superseded, a late signal applied at the desk's time, refused signals, buffer and step bounds, the lane view feeding the nowcast, the lane cycle time, constructor validation, no identity fields, a 5-seed random-signal fuzz accounting for every second, and the reference day's Visitors staffing replayed as session signals).
- Security review (independent reviewer, baseline): FAIL on the first pass, fixed: a drained per-desk queue kept its grown array (900 MB for 300 flooded desks; Medium), now trimmed when it drains; one desk's future-dated flood filled the engine's buffer and turned every other desk Unknown (Medium), now each desk holds at most 1,000 signals, a tenth of them ahead, and past half the buffer an equal share; signals of any age were accepted (a replayed backlog landed in one minute), now refused beyond 15 minutes behind the watermark; desks without a login source closed the moment the staff zone read empty and transaction-only desks were never Idle, now staffed within T2 of the staff leaving or of the last transaction (Proposed, in F10); sensor-derived states made healthy lanes Degraded, now counted instead; presence after a logout was not recorded; the minute end overflowed at DateTime.MaxValue; a far clock jump emitted every minute of the gap, now a desk more than a day behind skips it, counted. Notes for ARV-034 recorded in F10 (deduplicate by SourceEventId, map AMAN to sessions and completed transactions only, heartbeats per source, reference time from the host clock, catch per message).
- Re-review: PASS (the flood, drain, MinValue, catch-up and MaxValue probes rerun; 300 adversarial fuzz runs with no exception or invariant failure; worst Advance call 6 ms).
