Its two Lows fixed too: an Idle kept by a recent transaction is no longer flagged sensor-derived, and MaxDesks x MaxBufferedSignalsPerDesk is validated at 20 million at most.
- Gates (after the fixes): 45 rule and engine tests in Desks; the full unit suite; security scan 0 errors; docs.


## 2026-10-02 ARV-034 Stream host
- Summary: the queue engine worker of Ariva.Api.Stream (`Ariva.Infra/Streaming/QueueStreamWorker`, enabled by `Stream:Enabled`): one raw consumer (group `ariva-stream.queue-engine`, range assignor so a zone's partition of every sensing topic lands on one instance) reads crossings, occupancy, intervals and track samples, merges them per partition in Ariva's receive-time order, and feeds one `ZoneProcessor` per zone (`Ariva.Core/Queueing/ZoneProcessor`: queue engine, bins, exit rate, a live row with queue length and nowcast at the end of each minute; every batch checked again, CWE-501). Snapshots of the queue engine, the bins, the exit rate and the desk engine (`Capture` and `Restore`, plain records for System.Text.Json, checked against the engines' bounds on restore). `StreamStore` writes each checkpoint in one transaction: binary COPY into staging tables and upserts of `queue_minute`, `queue_bin` and `desk_minute`, the zones' snapshots (`stream_zone_state`) and the partitions' next offsets (`stream_offset`); Kafka offsets are committed only after it, and on assignment the consumer starts from the saved offsets. Script 0018 creates `queue_minute`, `queue_bin`, `desk_minute`, `egate_minute`, the two stream tables and the 15-minute continuous aggregate `queue_minute_15m` (the PRD's "0003" predates scripts 0003 to 0017). Docs: overview (stream host), runbook 4.5b, wiki architecture.
- Also fixed (found by running the integration suite against Docker for the first time in this environment): the integration fixture's CA2100 build error from ARV-026; an unreadable value on a rider topic was skipped below the consume pipe and never dead-lettered (ARV-020's test failed): the value deserializer now gives null for unreadable JSON and the endpoint's dead-letter pipe (`UnreadableFilter`) sends the raw record to the dead-letter topic, Kafka records only; the Kafka pipeline test now waits up to 4 minutes for a cold broker and asserts readiness.
- Desk worker: the desk engine's snapshot and `desk_minute` writing are in place; the worker that feeds it from the AMAN feed and desk zones is ARV-049 (desk sessions), and `egate_minute` is filled there too.
- Gotchas: the merge spans every assigned partition, not one partition's four topics: after partitions are added a zone's older records stay on the partition the old count gave, and a replay must apply them before its newer ones; a partition that starts at its saved or committed offset has no position until a record arrives, so "drained" is measured from the starting offset; the four topics must be merged in receive order, or a replay (which fetches the topics at different rates) offers a batch after a later batch of another topic has moved the watermark, and its events turn late; the reference clock is the batches' receive time, not the host's clock, so replays step identically; a zone idle while the consumer is caught up moves on by wall time (`Stream:IdleTickSeconds`, 0 in tests), the one input not from the records; an empty partition has no position until fetched, so "drained" also accepts a log whose low and high watermarks are equal.
- Gates: 11 unit tests (a zone restored from JSON after every batch gives the outputs of an uninterrupted run; the reference evening's Visitors nowcast in the live rows; final bins; refused batches; the receive-time cap; refused snapshots; queue and desk engine snapshots restored at random points in a fuzz continue identically) and 3 integration tests on Kafka and TimescaleDB (a run stopped mid-evening, its Kafka offsets deleted, more data produced while down, then restarted, writes exactly the minute and bin rows of a run that never stopped; the store's upserts are idempotent, the snapshot round-trips and the continuous aggregate refreshes; unreadable and wrong-key records are dead-lettered).
- Security review (independent reviewer, CWE-120 and CWE-501): FAIL on the first pass (one High, five Medium), fixed over four rounds: a poison interval record (corrected time near the calendar's start) stopped the whole host on every restart, now every event is checked against the canonical rules and a batch the engine still refuses is dead-lettered; stored snapshots were trusted (crash loops, rows refused by CHECKs), now `SnapshotChecks` validates zone, versions, counts (at most 10^12) and times before restore and a failing snapshot starts the zone afresh; the merge buffer was bounded by records only, now also by bytes and batches are trimmed; outputs were drained before the write, so a cancelled checkpoint lost rows, now `Peek` then write then `Acknowledge`, and a database outage keeps the outputs and pauses reading; the runtime role could write the continuous aggregate and rewrite Final bins, now revoked and a trigger keeps Final revisions immutable; co-partitioning was assumed, now checked at start and on each rebalance, records off the partition their key hashes to (CRC-32, every count the topics have had, recorded in `stream_partition_count`) are dead-lettered, the merge spans all assigned partitions so a scale-out keeps each zone in order, and partitions added are noticed within 30 seconds. Re-review: PASS (probes on real Kafka and TimescaleDB: poison record, stop during a checkpoint, database paused, misplaced record, hostile snapshots, partitions added while running and a recompute afterwards, the real producer's partitioning). Open Lows: a database restored from before a scale-out needs its earlier partition count (runbook 4.5b); the global merge lets one lagging partition delay an instance's zones until it drains or the bounds force a release; a checkpoint that keeps failing is logged and pauses reading but has no health signal yet (for ARV-063 or the operations work).
- Gates (after the fixes): 25 unit tests in the zone, pipeline and checksum classes, the full unit suite, 12 stream integration tests on Kafka and TimescaleDB plus the Kafka pipeline tests; security scan 0 errors; docs.
- After the review, running the whole integration suite against a cold broker showed the ARV-020 bus never recovering when it starts before its topics exist (Main provisions them in the background; the v8 rider faults on an unknown topic for good, and a restart of the bus left the endpoint degraded): `KafkaTopicsReady` now holds a host's start, before the bus, until the topics its rider consumes exist (`Kafka:Consumers:StartWaitSeconds`, 300), then starts the bus anyway with an error logged. The Kafka pipeline tests pass on a fresh broker in 14 seconds (they waited 4 minutes and failed before).


## 2026-10-02 ARV-035 Live snapshots and the SignalR hub
- Summary: after each checkpoint the queue stream worker publishes each zone's latest live row (`LiveZoneSnapshot`: queue length, nowcast, throughput, no-service reason, degraded flags; no identities) to Redis (`Ariva.Infra/Live/LiveSnapshots.cs`: key `{instance}live:zone:{zoneKey}` kept a day, channel `{instance}live:zones`, 4 KB at most, every value read back checked, CWE-501). Ariva.Api.Main maps `LiveHub` at `/hubs/live` (WebSockets only, MessagePack and JSON, 8 KB messages, one invocation at a time, the session filter and sweeper of ADR-0026, the SignalR Redis backplane under `{instance}signalr`). Connecting needs the new `LiveQueue.View` (Border shift supervisor, Terminal duty manager, Handler station manager; administrators through All); `JoinZone` re-reads the stored grant and sites on every call and needs the zone in the site's published profile (`LiveZoneDirectory`), answering the same `forbidden` otherwise, and returns the kept snapshot. `LiveRelay` subscribes to the channel and, on the replica holding the Redis lease `{instance}live:relay`, sends each snapshot to the zone's group. The access token is read from `access_token` only on `/hubs`, only without an Authorization header, once and at most 4 KB. `SiteAccessResolver` (sites by user id) now backs `SiteScope` and the hub. Docs: overview (live push), wiki 03, 04 (Redis, `web-hubs` ingress), 10 (runbook 4.8a), 13, cwe-controls.
- Also fixed (found by the first full e2e run with a database, Redis and Kafka in this environment): Ariva.Api.Ingest never registered `ISvcDeviceGateway` (only Main did; unit tests replaced it with fakes, which also adds it), so every device request answered 500 since ARV-022. `AddArivaSensingIngest` registers it, and `HostServicesTests` builds every controller and hub of every Backplane host from the host's own registrations (it fails on main for Ingest). The e2e suite gained Kafka (`ARIVA_E2E_KAFKA_BOOTSTRAP`; CI starts `apache/kafka:4.1.0`, required in CI) because Ingest accepts a push only once it is in Kafka; the device suites each have their own step-up administrator (a TOTP code counts once per account, so parallel suites refused each other); the device-health suite's profile gained the exit line validation now requires.
- Gotchas: SignalR clients must use WebSockets with skipNegotiation, or several Main replicas need sticky sessions; with skipNegotiation a refused WebSocket shows only as a failed connection, not a 401. `HubOptions.HubFilters` is internal (the wiring test reads it by reflection). The security scanner's minimal-API check reads a statement up to its first line ending in a semicolon, so the hub's options moved to `LiveHub.Configure`.
- Gates: 21 live hub unit tests (joins, refusals for grant, site and unknown zone, invalid keys including a trailing newline, 64-zone cap, 120 joins a minute, snapshot checks, the connection gate by query token and header with and without the grant, repeated and misplaced query tokens, the session filter and sweeper wired), the session connection cap, host service resolution for every host, 1 Redis integration test, 4 e2e tests (no token, forged token, cross-site and unknown zone, a snapshot published to Redis reaching an authorised client and an implausible or other zone's one not), the chart check; the full unit suite; the full e2e suite 265 passed with Postgres, Redis and Kafka; security scan 0 errors; docs.
- Security review (independent reviewer, CWE-862, CWE-863, CWE-384): FAIL on the first pass (three Medium, two Low), fixed: the WebSocket URL's token reached the ingress access and ModSecurity audit logs, now `/hubs` has its own ingress (`web-hubs`, `tokenInQuery`) with neither log and only critical errors, enforced by the chart security test; any well-formed key of a held site could be joined and joins were unbounded (each new group is a Redis subscription), now only published queue zones, 120 joins a minute per connection and 8 connections per session per replica; the connection gate test passed on a 500, now the gate is tested with and without the grant (mutation-checked); nothing tested that the live hub closes revoked sessions' connections, now a host test (mutation-checked); the zone key regex accepted a trailing newline (`$`), now `\z`. Re-review: PASS. Advisories: when handler tenancy lands (v1) `JoinZone` must also filter by zone owner; `ThroughputPerMinute` is not yet on the data boundary's list of fields that may cross ("To confirm"); separate Redis ACL users for Stream (writes `live:*`) and Main (reads, subscribes) would narrow who can push snapshots; ARV-055 must render the zone key as text and connect to the same-origin `/hubs/live`, never through the `api-main` host, whose ingress logs every request line (the chart check only sees ingresses that list `/hubs`).

## 2026-10-02 ARV-036 Golden replay
- Summary: a replay of the archive reproduces what the stream computed, and an exported replay is tamper-evident. To make the replay complete, a zone's device liveness now follows from its own records: Ingest keys `DeviceHealthReported` by the zone key (with a `Commissioned` flag) and marks events of an unreliable clock (`SensedFlags.ClockUnreliable`), the queue worker merges `ariva.device.health.v1` as a fifth co-partitioned topic (as `DeviceStatusBatch`), and `ZoneProcessor` (`DeviceLiveness`) records a device silent beyond 180 seconds or reporting itself offline as out (`DeviceOutage`, written to `zone_outage`), degrades the zone's live minutes and marks its bins (F11 implementation note); snapshot version 2 carries the liveness. The Stream host archives health reports (`device_health_event`, script 0019). `Ariva.Core/Queueing/Replay`: archive rows back into batches in the stream's order (`ReplayInput`, `ArchivedBatch`), `ZoneReplay` (a fresh zone processor per zone, settled after the range without watching devices), `ReplayLedger` (two SHA-256 chains over inputs and outputs, one canonical JSON line each, `Verify`). `ReplayRunner` reads the archive for a site, zones, range and profile version (published or retired, `ZoneGeometrySource`) and records each run in `replay_run`, whose time, login and chain link the database sets (trigger, append-only for the runtime role). `Ariva.Api.Stream --replay ...` and `--verify-replay=path` (`ReplayCommand`). Docs: overview, adapters, formulas F11, wiki 03, 04, 10 (runbook 4.5b and 4.10), 13, 16, `/replay`.
- Found and fixed on the way: every crossing and track Ingest published with a track id failed the canonical rules in the stream, because Ingest namespaces ids by device (`S-15/7`) and the rules allow no slash; the stream counted them invalid and computed no entries or exits from tracked devices since ARV-034 (its tests built batches without Ingest). The zone processor now checks the device's own part of the id (and the archive's day pseudonyms) and keys tracks by `<device>/<own part>`. The golden replay, which runs Ingest's own ingest, is what showed it.
- Golden values: the reference evening (seed 9303, S-15, S-17 and S-50 from 17:00 to 20:30 through Ingest) replays to: Visitors nowcast above 15 minutes first at 18:05; S-17 out from 18:20 (last heard) to 18:31 (heard again), Visitors degraded live while out and its 18:15 and 18:30 bins `Degraded`; island C's 19:00, 19:15 and 19:30 bins above 15 minutes; output hash `451f62ed…` with 2,083 outputs (`Ariva.UnitTests/Replay/replay-golden.json`, created in this story; it needs the human's approval to change). The same evening written to the TimescaleDB archive (pseudonymous tracks) and replayed by the command's runner under the demo seed's version 12 gives the same hash.
- Gotchas: replay equals the live run except for ties between records with the same receive time (offset order live, kind and id in the replay) and tracks across UTC midnight (daily pseudonyms); the live degraded flag starts when the silence passes the limit, while the outage and bin marks start at the minute the device was last heard; the geometry must include the overflow band (it changes the outputs); `ZoneOutputs` gained `Outages` with a default so existing callers compile.
- Gates: unit tests for liveness (silence, offline report, open outage at a replay's end, forgetting, uncommissioned devices, snapshot round trip during an outage, version 1 snapshots, hostile device states and codes, no repeated recomputations, track ids as Ingest and the archive write them, settings), the golden replay (the three scripted events, the golden hash run after run and after a fresh ingest, settings change the hash, the export verifies and fails when a line is changed, dropped, added, moved, duplicated, given an extra property or too long), the command (arguments, export file, printed row hash); integration tests on TimescaleDB (the archived evening gives the golden hash, refused requests leave no record, the runtime role cannot change or remove the record or backdate, unchain or redirect a run, even with a temporary table named like it) and on Kafka (a legacy device-keyed health record is skipped, an unreadable one dead-lettered); the full unit (1,522), integration and e2e (264 passed, one flaky functional) suites; security scan 0 errors; docs.
- Security review (independent reviewer, baseline with CWE-501, CWE-89, CWE-400, CWE-73, CWE-345, CWE-269): FAIL on the first pass (three Medium, five Low), fixed: one Kafka record with a device code the snapshot checks refused made the zone's snapshot unrestorable (now one registry rule for codes in the stream, snapshots and both archives); the replay record's time and author came from the client and the runtime role could backdate a run (now set by a trigger, with a chain of row hashes); `Verify` accepted unhashed extra or duplicate properties (now every line must be exactly as written); in-memory bounds above the pod's memory and an unbounded line read (now a million rows per zone and a bounded reader); rows sharing a batch id but not a kind could abort a replay (now grouped by the whole batch identity, no casts); device times of the calendar's start passed the snapshot checks; no test of the export file's creation; an upgrade would have dead-lettered every health record keyed by device id (now skipped and counted). Second pass: FAIL on two Lows (the trigger's table lookup could be redirected by a temporary table, now schema-qualified with a pinned search path; the command did not print the row hash). Re-review: PASS, nothing open. Trust assumption, documented: the archive and `replay_run` are as trustworthy as the runtime database login.

## 2026-10-02 CI: pipefail in jobs with their own defaults
- The web and e2e jobs set `defaults.run.working-directory`, which replaces the workflow's `defaults.run.shell: bash`, so their steps ran under `bash -e` without pipefail and `npx playwright test | tee e2e.log` passed whatever the tests did: the e2e job on main was green with 6 failed tests (the device suites, broken since ARV-022 and fixed in ARV-035). Both jobs name `shell: bash` again.

## 2026-10-02 ARV-037 Alert rules
- Summary: `AlertRule` (`Ariva.Core/Domain/Entities/AlertRule.cs`) is typed data only: metric (`Nowcast`, `BinP90`, `QueueLength`, `OverflowOccupied`, `SensorOffline`, `DesksBelowPlan`), comparator (`IsTrue` only for the two conditions), threshold in the metric's unit (minutes up to 600, people up to 100,000, desks up to 1,000), optional minimum queue (nowcast only), clear threshold strictly on the clearing side, sustain and clear minutes (1 to 120), severity, owner role, escalation after 1 to 1,440 minutes to a role or a named function, email flag, enabled. `AlertRuleValues.Problems()` checks every value; names, zones and contacts refuse control and format characters. `SvcAlertRules` and `api/v1/admin/alert-rules` (`[SiteScoped]`): search, get, create, update, delete within the caller's sites (another site's rule answers 404), enums by exact name only, zones must be queue or overflow zones of the site's published profile, a caller who is not an administrator can neither give, change nor remove a role they do not hold, codes R-001 onwards per site under an advisory lock (deleted rules keep theirs; at most 500 live rules and R-999999), every change audited with JSON summaries (fixed fields first, then the zones' count and SHA-256, then the zones). Deleting is a critical action (step-up MFA, critical-actions.json). Permissions: Border and Terminal write, Handler reads, administrators everything. Script 0020 creates `alert_rule` with checks that repeat the entity's and a trigger that keeps a rule's id, site and code; the runtime role cannot delete or truncate. The demo seed adds R-001 to R-005 of the prototype (`DemoAlertRules`) once, each audited, only when the published profile has the zones they watch. Docs: wiki 11 section 8 (API and fields), glossary, cwe-controls, decisions.
- Found and fixed on the way: the session hides soft-deleted rows everywhere (a filter and a where clause), so a code query through it reused a deleted rule's code (unique violation) and the seed's "no rule at all" check would have re-seeded a site whose rules were all deleted; both read the table with SQL now (the integration tests found it).
- Gates: unit tests (typed values and 32 refused cases, hostile names, no expression member, conditions, codes, audit summary clipped at the trail's limit, the seed's rules mapped to the reference scenario raise exactly its own alerts), the permission matrix (5 rows) and critical actions; integration tests on TimescaleDB (round trip, codes after deletes and under 6 concurrent creates, site scope for every action, unknown and service zones, roles given or taken without holding them, exact enum names, the seed not bringing back deleted rules, the runtime role refused for deletes and out-of-range or identity-changing updates, demo seed counts and audit); e2e `alert-rules.spec.ts` (seed, CRUD, step-up on delete, typed values, hostile names, scope, handler read-only); the full unit, integration (144 passed, 1 skipped) and e2e (275 passed) suites; security scan 0 errors; docs.
- Security review (independent reviewer, CWE-94, CWE-862, CWE-863 and the baseline): FAIL on the first pass (one High, two Medium, six Low), fixed: deleting a rule was not a step-up critical action though the PRD lists it (now `[RequiresRecentMfa]` and listed); `Enum.TryParse` accepted padded and comma-joined names, OR-ing them into another value (now exact names only); the audit summary put the zones first, so the trail's 2,000-character clip could hide owner and enabled changes, and names could forge `k=v` pairs (now JSON, fixed fields first); a caller could strip roles they do not hold; the database checks were weaker than the entity's (now repeated, plus the identity trigger); the code space could be exhausted at R-9999 (now R-999999); the e2e role case was masked by unknown zones; the seed wrote no per-rule audit and ignored which profile was published. Also from its advisories: only queue and overflow zones can be watched; the wiki says the contact names a function, never a person. Re-review: PASS. Open: whether disabling or weakening a rule needs step-up too (decisions.md; settle before ARV-039); handler tenancy and row-level security on alert tables, LIKE wildcards in the search text, and the evaluator treating names and contacts as data only (ARV-038, ARV-039).

## 2026-10-03 ARV-038 Rule evaluation and backtest
- Summary: `AlertEvaluator` (`Ariva.Core/Alerting`) is the prototype's rule engine (`ScenarioDay.EvaluateRule`) as a pure fold over one target's minutes: a minute without a value is skipped, the condition (with the nowcast's minimum queue) must hold for the sustain minutes in a row to raise, a raised target is disarmed (one open alert per rule and target), and the clear condition (beyond the clear threshold on the clearing side, or the condition ending) must hold for the clear minutes to resolve and re-arm. `AlertInputs` reads what the stream stored: the nowcast and queue length per minute (`queue_minute`; a nowcast is skipped while the queue length is degraded, not for an exit-rate-only throughput, or no nowcast rule could fire before ARV-049), the largest P90 among bins that ended within 150 minutes (`queue_bin`), device outages (`zone_outage` and devices offline in the registry), and the predicted nowcast. Targets are the rule's queue and overflow zones of the published profile, or their commissioned devices. Ariva.Api.Stream evaluates every enabled rule once a minute (`AlertEvaluation`, `Alerts:Evaluation`): one replica under `pg_try_advisory_xact_lock(38, 0)`, each rule in its own unit of work (`AlertRuleTick`), raises insert `alert` rows that keep what the rule said, clears resolve them, and `alert_rule_state` keeps each target's fold between ticks (script 0021). A new target starts at the present; an edit restarts the counts (detected by a hash of the rule's values); a metric change, a target leaving the rule and a withdrawn rule resolve their open alerts (`RuleChanged`, `TargetWithdrawn`, `RuleWithdrawn`). `POST api/v1/admin/alert-rules/backtest` judges a rule (saved or not, checked like a create) over an ended range of up to 24 hours in the last 90 days with the same fold: count, first raise, the alerts (at most 200) and targets with data. The predicted-breach metric `PredictedNowcast` (lead 15 to 60 minutes) steps the queue a minute at a time with the arrival wave and the current throughput and takes the highest nowcast within the lead (formulas.md); it reads `IArrivalWaveSource`, which is empty until ARV-047. Docs: wiki 11 section 8, 03, 04, 10 (4.8b), 12, 16, glossaries, formulas (predicted breach), overview.
- Decisions: `OverflowOccupied` and `DesksBelowPlan` rules have nothing to judge yet (the stream stores no overflow band occupancy, and storing it would change the golden replay's hash, which needs the human's approval; the staffing plan is ARV-049). `BinP90` judges a bin's latest revision, so a backtest after bins became final can differ from what was judged live. The predicted nowcast takes the peak over the horizon, not the value at its end, because a long lead otherwise lets the desks "clear" a wave that peaks earlier (found by the 60-minute test).
- Gates: unit tests (sustain, skipped minutes, dedupe, auto-resolve by clear threshold and by the condition, minimum-queue gate, below comparators, conditions, repeated minutes, live one minute at a time with the state stored as JSON equals the backtest on random series, R-001 raises at 18:05 on the replayed Visitors minutes live and backtested, at the same minute as the prototype; predicted wait and its lead; host services for every hosted service and the Stream evaluation); integration tests on TimescaleDB (the evening's stream outputs written by the stream store, the live evaluation ticked every minute from 17:00 to 20:35: R-001 at 18:05, R-003 on S-17 18:20 to 18:31, R-004 and R-005 on island C, and every rule's backtest equal to the stored alerts; a repeated tick changes nothing; a held lock skips the tick; a raise, clear and raise in one tick; a value beyond what an alert keeps; targets leaving and metric changes; new targets start at the present; backtest bounds, site scope and the live-queue requirement; the runtime role cannot delete or rewrite alerts; list parameters bind as data); e2e backtest (shape, bounds, lead times, permissions); the full unit, integration (160 passed, 1 skipped) and e2e (277 passed) suites; security scan 0 errors; docs.
- Security review (independent reviewer, baseline): FAIL on the first pass (one High, three Medium, four Low), fixed: a target raised, cleared and raised again within one tick hit the one-open-alert index (the session inserts before it updates) and, all rules sharing one transaction, stopped every rule for good (now a flush after each clear, and each rule in its own transaction); a stored value beyond the alert table's range did the same (now recorded at the bound); open alerts of targets that left a rule never resolved; backtests had no concurrency limit and the P90 look-up scanned every bin per minute (now two at a time per host and a binary search); new and re-enabled targets replayed three hours of history; untrimmed zones made a backtest silently empty; the new list binding had no test (now an integration test and an error for an empty list); the backtest showed past values to a caller who cannot see live queues. Second pass: FAIL on one High it had missed in the first round: the Stream host never registered what the evaluation resolves per rule, so every tick would have failed in production (the tests built their own host); now registered by `AddArivaAlertEvaluation`, with host tests that every hosted service builds and that the Stream host resolves the evaluation's services (mutation-checked). Third pass: PASS. Advisory: the backtest limit is per host, not per user (the limiter runs before authentication).

## 2026-10-03 Chore: OpenTelemetry 1.19
- OpenTelemetry.Exporter.OpenTelemetryProtocol and Extensions.Hosting 1.14.0 to 1.19.1, the AspNetCore, Http and Runtime instrumentations to 1.19.0: 1.14.0 carried four moderate advisories (NU1902 on every host). The build has no NU1902 warning left; backend gate, health, security baseline and log redaction e2e pass.

## 2026-10-03 ARV-039 Alert lifecycle
- Summary: `api/v1/alerts` (`AlertsController`, `SvcAlerts`): search (site, state by exact name, open, zone, rule, raise-time range; newest first), get (with `escalationDueUtc`), acknowledge, escalate and resolve with a note. Permissions `Alert.View`, `Alert.Search` and `Alert.Edit` for the three operational roles (administrators have all). An alert is visible and actionable only within the caller's sites and where the caller's role is responsible (`Alert.IsFor`: the owner role; the escalation role once escalated; every role when the rule has no owner; administrators), otherwise 404. Transitions move forward only: acknowledged once (from Raised or Escalated), escalated once (from Raised or Acknowledged), resolved by hand with a note (up to 500 characters, no control or invisible characters) or by itself; anything else answers 409. An action checks visibility, then locks the alert's row, then loads and checks again, so neither the evaluation tick nor another person is overwritten; the tick locks too before it clears, withdraws or escalates. The Stream tick escalates alerts still Raised past their rule's escalation minutes. Every action and escalation is audited as JSON. Script 0022 adds the lifecycle columns, checks and a trigger that refuses any backward move, a second acknowledgement or escalation, writing one without entering its state, and a manual resolution without a note. Both hosts publish an `AlertNotice` (no people) on the Redis channel `live:alerts` after commit; the live relay checks it (CWE-501) and sends it to the hub groups `alerts:{site}:{role}` of the responsible roles, which a screen joins with `JoinAlerts(site)` (needs `Alert.View`, the site, and the user's stored roles). Docs: wiki 12 (alerts table and API), 11, 03, 10 (4.8b), 13, glossary, overview.
- Found on the way: an alert acknowledged, then escalated, then acknowledged again would have rewritten the first acknowledgement (now acknowledged once; the escalation role resolves an alert handed over to it). NHibernate's LINQ `Any` loaded the entity into the session, so a load after the row lock returned the copy from before the lock; the pre-lock visibility check is a count (the concurrent-acknowledge test caught it).
- Gates: unit tests (transitions, once-only, due escalation, responsibility, notes, hub alert joins and refusals, notice routing and plausibility, the matrix with 5 new rows, every hosted service and the Stream evaluation's services resolve); integration tests on TimescaleDB (visibility per role and site, the full acknowledge, escalate and resolve path with audit and notices, automatic escalation once, two people acknowledging at once, alerts without owner, the tick waiting for a row locked by an acknowledgement or a manual resolution, a note that looks like other audit fields, the runtime role refused for every backward move or rewrite) and on Redis (notices announced, forged ones dropped); e2e `alerts.spec.ts` (visibility per role and site, each transition and its refusals, 409s, notes, live hub routing by role, forged notices dropped, no people in notices); the full unit, integration (179 passed, 1 skipped) and e2e (286 passed) suites; security scan 0 errors; docs. No alert screen exists yet (ARV-055), so there are no functional Playwright tests for this story.
- Security review (independent reviewer, CWE-862, CWE-863 and the baseline): FAIL on the first pass (two Medium, one Low): the trigger did not enforce acknowledged once, escalated once and a note on a manual resolution; audit summaries concatenated free text (CWE-117); no test covered the tick's row lock (both new tests fail when it is removed). Also taken: no lock on an alert the caller cannot see, the relay subscribing each channel once, the escalation limit as a parameter. Re-review: PASS; its one new Low (acknowledgement or escalation fields written without entering the state) fixed in the trigger with tests. Advisory: alerts without an owner role (R-003) are visible to every role of the site, handlers included, until handler-level scoping exists.

## 2026-10-03 ARV-040 Email notifications
- Summary: alert emails as a transactional outbox. The host that changes an alert (Stream when the evaluation raises or escalates it, Main when someone escalates it) writes one `email_message` row per alert, kind and recipient in that transaction (`AlertEmails`), only when `Email:Enabled` and the rule notifies by email: on a raise to the people of the owner role (every operational role without owner), on an escalation to the people of the escalation role; people are enabled, not break-glass, hold the role and the alert's site, and have one plain address (others are skipped and logged). Ariva.Api.Integration, the only host with egress to the mail relay, sends what is due (`EmailSender`, `EmailSenderWorker`) through MailKit: one replica at a time (transaction advisory lock), at most `Email:MaxPerMinute` over all of them, rows claimed with `FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED`, each re-checked at send time, retried after 1, 2, 4 ... 60 minutes and given up after `Email:MaxAttempts`. Templates `AlertRaised` and `AlertEscalated` are embedded in Ariva.Resources; every value is one line of printable text before it goes in, the subject is one line, MimeKit encodes the headers, one recipient per message (CWE-93). Limits: `Email:MaxPerRecipientPerHour` (20) per address, counting what the same transaction already wrote; past it the row is written as Suppressed. Script 0023: the table with checks, unique per alert, kind and lower-case address, the runtime role may update only the sending columns and never delete, and a trigger keeps what an email says and to whom and makes Sent, Suppressed and Failed final. The users API now refuses an email that is not one plain address. Settings: `Email` (base, every host: on or off, sender, limits) and `Email:Smtp` (Integration only: relay, TLS, credential; the password is never printed; clear text only with `AllowInsecure`, refused outside vm-local and k8s-dev). smtp4dev: compose in development (pinned 3.15.0) and the pinned local .NET tool (`.config/dotnet-tools.json`) in E2E, so no image pull.
- Decisions (best practice, recorded in decisions.md): send from Integration, the egress host the network design already gives the mail relay (wiki 05, flow 15), not Main; delivery at least once; emails name the escalation role, never the rule's free-text contact; no officer names or notes in emails. Open: retention for staff addresses in `email_message`, and whether changing a user's email (which redirects their alert emails) should need step-up.
- Gates: unit tests (templates from the resources name only values an alert gives; values with line breaks, bidi and zero-width characters stay on one line; addresses; the wire message has one recipient and no added header; settings; the password never printed; clear text only in vm-local and k8s-dev; the outbox host holds no relay; retry, give-up and final states; Integration sends, Main only writes); integration tests on TimescaleDB with an SMTP server in the test process (`SmtpSink`): recipients by role and site, not the other site, disabled, malformed, display-name or break-glass accounts; no email when the rule does not notify; ownerless alerts; automatic and manual escalation; dedupe; the hourly limit across alerts and within one tick for raises and escalations; refused recipient retried and given up; unreachable relay; per-minute limit and rows held by another replica skipped; a replica holding the round lock makes the other skip; a recipient disabled or moved after writing is held back; the users API refuses unusable addresses; the runtime role cannot delete or rewrite an email; e2e `alert-emails.spec.ts` against smtp4dev (addresses refused and kept, a rule that notifies, an escalation sends the duty manager exactly one email with the rule in a one-line subject, no Cc, Bcc or Reply-To, one To, no notes, row Sent once); the full unit, integration (205 passed, 1 skipped) and e2e (288 passed) suites; security scan 0 errors; docs.
- Security review (independent reviewer, CWE-501, CWE-93 and the baseline): FAIL on the first pass (two Medium, two Low), fixed: the hourly limit only counted committed rows, so one tick raising many alerts could queue far past it (now a grouped count plus what the transaction already wrote; the new test fails without it); the runtime role could rewrite a pending email's recipient and body, and the sender trusted the row (now column grants, a trigger, and an eligibility re-check at send time); the relay password was meant for every host and a record's `ToString` would print it (now `Email:Smtp`, Integration only, redacted); the tool manifest was git-ignored, so E2E could not start in CI (now committed). Also taken from its advisories: one sending replica at a time so the per-minute limit holds over all of them, no free-text contact in emails, addresses compared without case, smtp4dev pinned in compose. Re-review: PASS. Remaining advisories: the runtime role can still insert rows and change user addresses (limited to Ariva's own users); two concurrent writers can each pass the hourly limit once; sends happen inside the claiming transaction (at least once, runbook 4.8c).

## 2026-10-03 ARV-041 Flights and allocations model
- Summary: the Flight Demand model. `FlightLeg` (one per site and the feed's flight key; direction fixed) keeps its schedule with the time of the message that set it and each milestone (estimate, actual, on-block, gate open, boarding, off-block, cancelled, diverted) with the time of the most recent message that reported it, so feeds may arrive in any order and end in the same state; an older message can still fill a milestone nobody reported; the status follows from the milestones; a change raises `FlightChanged` (`ariva.flight.flight-changed.v1`) through the outbox. `FlightEvent` is the append-only record of reported milestones. `CounterAllocation` gives a departing leg check-in counters at a check-in checkpoint, resolving the AODB's counter codes through the desk code mappings and keeping unmapped codes apart. `FeedFreshness` and the stale-feed alarm: every minute Integration judges each feed of each site (`FeedFreshnessRule`: Stale when silent beyond its cadence while flights are due at the site, Idle when none are, SSIM never stale) and reports changes as a warning and in the `Ariva.Flights` metrics (`ariva.feeds.stale`, `ariva.feeds.went_stale`, `ariva.feeds.recovered`, `ariva.flights.items`). Every adapter of ARV-043 to ARV-046 will call `ISvcFlightIntake` (legs, events, allocations, at most 500 items, one transaction per call): each item is checked by `FlightRules` (code shapes, exact enum names, UTC and time windows, bounded counts, microsecond times) before it touches an entity (CWE-501) and a bad item never stops the others; all the call's legs are locked up front in ascending key order (class 41), so concurrent feeds neither double-create a leg nor deadlock; only a call with an item that passed the checks refreshes its feed. Script 0024: four tables with checks that repeat the rules; the runtime role cannot delete legs, allocations or freshness rows, nor change or delete the event record.
- Decisions: the stale-feed alarm is a system alarm (state, log, metrics; runbook 3 and 4.3), not a user alert rule metric: alert rules watch zones and a feed is not a zone. Flights due are counted per site. Carried to ARV-043 to ARV-046: site and feed from the client binding and the adapter, never the message; feed precedence and which feeds may cancel or divert; seeding the expected feeds per site; lock-table sizing for full batches; flight data retention (wiki 14, To confirm).
- Gates: unit tests (good legs normalised; 31 kinds of bad leg refused; calendar-edge times return errors, never throw; the same final state for all six orders of three messages; newest milestone kept and missing ones filled; status from milestones, cancel and reinstatement by message time; milestones of the other direction and direction changes refused; FlightChanged only on a change; exact event names; allocations with unmapped codes and older messages; the stale-feed rule; feed names and message times); integration tests on TimescaleDB (per-item results and message-time ordering with FlightChanged in the outbox; whole-call refusals; events of known legs only, all recorded; allocations resolved through mappings and refused for arrivals, other checkpoints and unknown legs; six feeds creating one leg at once make one row; two feeds sending 40 legs in opposite orders never deadlock; edge-time items never stop a batch; sites keep their own legs and checkpoints; Fresh, Stale, Idle and recovery with the sweep's own lock; refused-only batches do not refresh a feed; the database repeats the rules for the runtime role); the full unit, integration (226 passed, 1 skipped) and e2e (288 passed) suites; security scan 0 errors; docs.
- Security review (independent reviewer, CWE-501 and the baseline): FAIL on the first pass (three Medium, one Low), fixed: a time at the edge of the calendar made the check throw and failed the whole batch (now a check that cannot overflow, with tests); a leg lock could collide with the sweep's leader lock and suppress the alarm for every site (now separate lock classes and leg keys computed in C#); no test covered two sites using the same flight key (now tested, and the mapping query filters every table by site); two feeds could deadlock on legs locked in item order (now all locks up front in key order, tested). Also taken: refused-only batches no longer keep a feed fresh, microsecond times, no deletes of legs. Re-review: PASS.

## 2026-10-03 ARV-042 Integration clients and TOTP authentication
- Summary: `IntegrationClient` (script 0025): client id `ic_` and 26 base32 characters; secret `ics_` and 256 random bits, stored as a PBKDF2-SHA256 hash; TOTP seed protected with its own Data Protection purpose; scopes (`flights:write`, `allocations:write`, `immigration:write`, `sensing:write`, `queues:read`, `displays:read`), 1 to 32 bound sites, 1 to 16 required source networks, the per-request TOTP policy (on by default for immigration clients), a token version, the replay guard step, the failure count and lockout. Administration in Ariva.Api.Main (`api/v1/admin/integration-clients`: list, view, create, update, rotate secret, reset seed, disable, enable, unlock), every change a step-up critical action, audited without secrets, limited to the administrator's own sites; secret, seed and provisioning URI shown once. `POST /api/v1/auth` in Ariva.Api.Integration (AMAN shape): client id, secret and TOTP code for a 15-minute token of the separate integration audience and signing key, with the replay guard, per-address (20 a minute) and per-client (5 a minute) limits, a lockout after 10 failures for 15 minutes, and the same 401 `invalid_client` for every failure. The `Ariva.Integration` scheme re-checks each call against the client's record (known, active, same token version, inside the networks, `X-TOTP-Code` when the policy is on); `[IntegrationScope]` reads scopes and sites from the database and needs the `{siteCode}` route token to be one of them; every call, refused ones included, is recorded in `integration_call` with its payload's SHA-256. Two connectivity checks (`api/v1/integration/sites/{siteCode}/flights/check` and `immigration/check`) let integrators test scope and site binding before ARV-043. Docs: integration.md, decisions, wiki 04, 08, 10 (4.8d), 11.
- Decisions: data calls check `X-TOTP-Code` within the window without the replay guard (the exchange keeps it), so a client is not limited to one call per 30 seconds; a lock stops new exchanges only, so tokens already held run until they expire (an attacker on the client's network cannot cut a running feed by failing on purpose; disable stops a client at once); networks are required; clients are disabled, never deleted. Open: retention of `integration_call`; scope grants not tied to the granter's own permissions; per-IP partitions by full IPv6 address rather than /64; oversized bodies (413) leave no `integration_call` row.
- Gates: unit tests (credential formats, networks with host bits, `/0` and IPv4-mapped blocks refused, token version moved by every change, `ver` claim, scope handler theory, permission matrix with the integration access kind, the site-scope architecture rule for integration actions with its fixtures); integration tests on TimescaleDB (credentials shown once and stored protected, site-limited administrators, exchange once per code and the same refusal for everything else, lockout with the count reset and no relock after expiry, the per-client minute limit not counting toward the lockout, token versions and per-request codes, outside callers counting nothing, eight concurrent administrator changes moving the version eight times, the runtime role refused for rewriting the records); e2e `integration-auth.spec.ts` (register and shown once, exchange and refusals, scopes, sites, networks and cross-audience tokens, per-request codes, lockout, unlock, rotation and disable) with the log scan extended to integration secrets and seeds; the full unit, integration and e2e suites; security scan 0 errors; docs.
- Security review (independent reviewer, CWE-287, CWE-306, CWE-863, CWE-269): FAIL on the first pass (one High, three Medium, two Low), fixed: anyone who knew a client id could lock it out from anywhere (now networks are required and an outside exchange counts nothing, the count resets at the lock, an expired lock is not renewed by the same failure, a lock no longer stops tokens already held); tokens issued in the second of a change survived it (now a token version in every token, a conditional accept, scopes and sites read from the database); refused calls were not recorded (now recorded under their client with 401); the site-scope architecture test exempted integration actions entirely (now they may name a site only through the `{siteCode}` route token); the body was hashed before authentication; the e2e log scan did not look for integration secrets. Advisories taken: a separate seed protection purpose, required update fields, mapped IPv6 blocks refused, a bounded address column, TimeProvider in the audit middleware, no PBKDF2 for malformed ids. Re-review: PASS. Advisories after the re-review, taken: administrator changes lock the client's row so concurrent changes cannot write the same token version (tested; the test fails without the lock); the accepting update also requires the client not to be locked; the architecture rule recognises a class-level `[IntegrationScope]` and refuses a `siteCode` parameter bound from anywhere but the route. The SEC-052 allowlist entry for `POST /api/v1/auth` awaits approval.

## 2026-10-03 ARV-043 Integration API: flights and allocations
- Summary: `POST api/v1/integration/sites/{siteCode}/flights/batch`, `.../flights/events` (`flights:write`) and `.../allocations/batch` (`allocations:write`) in Ariva.Api.Integration (`FlightFeedController`). The site comes from the route and is checked against the client's sites before the action; the feed is the client's own (`api-` and its id), so neither comes from the body. A body is `{ messageTimeUtc?, items: [1 to 500] }`, `application/json` (415 otherwise), at most 1 MB (413, also when chunked; answered and recorded by the call audit when the length is known), read strictly by `BatchBody` (exact camelCase names, no unknown or repeated members, no comments or trailing commas, strict numbers, depth 8; a refusal names only members Ariva knows). Each item goes through `ISvcFlightIntake` (ARV-041) and is answered in order: 200 with `{ received, applied, unchanged, refused, items }`. `Idempotency-Key` is required: script 0026 (`integration_idempotency`, key per client) claims it in the transaction that applies the batch (`INSERT ... ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING`, so a concurrent retry waits for the first and then replays), stores the answer once, replays it with `Idempotent-Replayed: true`, refuses the same key with another body, operation or site (422), and frees it when the batch rolls back; keys live 24 hours and `IdempotencySweeper` deletes expired ones every 10 minutes; the runtime role cannot rewrite a claim or an answer or delete a live key. Limits: `integration-batch` concurrency policy (8 at once, 16 waiting per replica, before authentication and before any body is buffered) and `IntegrationClientRateLimiter` (120 batches a minute per client per replica, after the token is validated), both 429; the call audit buffers bodies within the endpoint limit in memory; the api-integration ingress takes at most 1 MB. Docs: integration.md, decisions, wiki 02, 04, 08 (endpoints, limits, errors, samples), 10 (4.8e), 14 (retention).
- Decisions: Ariva's API convention (data and RFC 9457 problems) rather than AMAN's `Result<T>` envelope, with per-item results and 200 even when items were refused; required keys with 422 on reuse (IETF Idempotency-Key draft); one feed per client; the site in the path of every Integration API endpoint.
- Gates: unit tests (strict parsing: 15 kinds of refused body, 500 and 501 items, no echo of unknown member names, offset times kept out of UTC; key shapes; feed names; content types; the answer's counts; the chunked and declared size limits; the per-client limiter); integration tests on TimescaleDB (replay and mismatch by body, operation and site; keys per client; a concurrent retry waits and replays; a rolled-back claim frees the key; expired keys claimed afresh; the sweep deletes only expired keys; the runtime role refused for rewriting answers or claims, deleting live keys and truncating); e2e `flights-api.spec.ts` (batches applied item by item, replay, 422, keys per client, concurrent retries applied once, events and allocations with unmapped counters, scope and site refusals, 13 malformed or oversized bodies refused without echo or effect, injection payloads refused per item with the table intact, the at-once limit refusing with nothing applied, the per-client limit with Retry-After, every call recorded with its hash); the full unit, integration and e2e suites; security scan 0 errors; docs.
- Security review (independent reviewer, CWE-501, CWE-120, CWE-89 and the baseline): FAIL on the first pass (one High, two Medium), fixed: one client could evict the integration pod with concurrent 1 MB batches spilling to the 64 MiB /tmp or exhausting memory (now the at-once policy before any body is read, in-memory buffering within the limit, a per-client allowance and a 1 MB ingress); no test that idempotency keys are per client (added, integration and e2e); no test of the size limit without a Content-Length (added). Also taken: body detection by `CanHaveBody` in the call audit. Re-review: PASS. Open, low: the at-once limit is shared by all clients of a replica (bounded by the per-client allowance; revisit when several AODBs share a site); calls refused by it are counted by the rate limiter, not `integration_call`.
- Backlog (Phase 1): a unit of work disposed without `EndAsync` commits what was promised (pre-existing; the API's filter ends it explicitly and rolls back on exceptions, and the batch endpoints rely on that); make disposal roll back.

## 2026-10-03 ARV-044 AIDX 22.1 inbound
- Summary: `POST api/v1/integration/sites/{siteCode}/aodb/aidx` (`flights:write`) takes one `IATA_AIDX_FlightLegNotifRQ` (`application/xml` or `text/xml`, 5 MB). `AidxReader` reads it twice from the buffered bytes: a non-validating pass that refuses any DOCTYPE and counts depth (32) and elements (200,000); then a validating pass into a document against Ariva's embedded AIDX 22.1 profile (`aidx-22.1-ariva-profile.xsd`: every element Ariva reads typed and bounded, the rest accepted unread through lax wildcards), with `DtdProcessing.Prohibit`, `XmlResolver = null`, no entity characters, no schema locations or inline schemas, 1 to 500 FlightLeg and a required UTC `TimeStamp` as the message time. `AidxMapping` places each leg on the site's side (airports of the site's terminals, IATA or ICAO), builds the key `RJ111-20261003-A`, maps operation times (ONB, OFB, TDN, TKO by SCT, EST, ACT), resources (actual over planned), aircraft type, codeshares and `DX`/`DV`; `SvcAidxIntake` applies the legs through `ISvcFlightIntake` as the client's feed with one result per leg at its index. The answer is an `IATA_AIDX_FlightLegRS` acknowledgement (`Success`, a `Warning` per refused or unchanged leg); 400 problems name only line and position. `Idempotency-Key` optional (script 0027 adds the `aodb.aidx` operation). Limits: the `integration-aidx` concurrency policy (2 at once, 4 waiting), and the client allowance now spent by the call audit before any body is read on every Integration API call. Ingress: api-integration back to 1 MB, a path-scoped `api-integration-feeds` ingress takes 5 MB under `/api/v1/integration/`. Docs: integration.md, decisions, wiki 04, 08 (section 8 mapping table and acknowledgement, section 9), 10 (4.8e).
- Decisions: Ariva's own AIDX profile instead of IATA's licensed XSDs (customers licensed for them can add them, To confirm); legs only, no `FlightEvent` rows (recorded with what is lost); the RS shape To confirm per AODB; ARV-044, ARV-045 and ARV-048 are built before the deferred ARV-029 emulators with the test as the partner.
- Gates: unit tests (22: a valid message, XXE, entity expansion, external DTD, profile breaks without echo, wrong root and namespace, 0 and 501 legs, missing and offset TimeStamp, depth, element count, size, processing instructions, schema location and inline schema, mapping both sides, ICAO and lower case, legs not of the site, DX and DV, offset times, the acknowledgement); integration test (legs applied at their own index, a site without airports refuses all); e2e `aidx.spec.ts` (applied and acknowledged, replay and 422 with a key, unchanged without one, a later message landing and cancelling, 11 malicious or malformed messages refused whole without echo or effect, scope, site and token refusals, calls recorded); the full unit (1786), integration and e2e (321) suites; security scan 0 errors; docs.
- Security review (independent reviewer, CWE-501, CWE-120 and the baseline including CWE-611 and CWE-776): FAIL on the first pass (one Medium, two Low), fixed: an AIDX message without TimeStamp and key was stamped with its receipt time, so a re-delivered old message could overwrite newer values (TimeStamp now required, docs corrected); no test of the element cap; no test that schema locations and inline schemas are ignored. Advisories taken: unreadable dates are 400s, airports compared in upper case, the 5 MB ingress limited to the feed paths, the client allowance spent before any body is read (so calls refused by scope or site cannot hold the AIDX permits), and the limiter resolved fail-closed. Re-review: PASS.

## 2026-10-03 CI: reserved ports for the E2E hosts
- The ARV-043 CI run failed before any test: Ariva.Api.Ingest could not bind 51002 (address already in use). The E2E hosts listen inside Linux's ephemeral port range, so an outgoing connection made while they start can take one of their ports. The e2e job now reserves 51000 to 51100 (and the smtp4dev ports) with `net.ipv4.ip_local_reserved_ports` before the suite. ARV-043's own tests passed locally (316 e2e); nothing in the code changed.

## 2026-10-03 ARV-045 Outbound endpoints and the ACRIS connector
- Summary: `OutboundEndpoint` (script 0028), administered in Ariva.Api.Main at `api/v1/admin/outbound-endpoints` with the integration permissions, every change a step-up critical action, audited without secrets, within the administrator's sites: purpose (`Generic` or `AcrisFlights` for one site), base URL, 1 to 16 networks, the authentication kind (fixed for life) with its non-secret settings, optional pinned CA, timeout, retries and breaker, and for ACRIS the pull path and interval. Secrets are protected with the Data Protection purpose `Ariva.Outbound.v1` and never returned; moving an endpoint (URL, networks, pinned CA, token path) needs the secret again. `OutboundRules` checks registrations (HTTPS or a vm-local lab host, no user information, query or fragment, same-origin token and pull paths, reserved headers, per-kind secret fields). `OutboundClients` builds one HttpClient per endpoint version: resilience (timeouts, idempotent retries with backoff, a circuit breaker), redirect refusal, the handler of the kind (AMAN-style TOTP client credentials, OAuth 2.0 client credentials with Basic client authentication, API key header, HMAC signature, mutual TLS), then `OutboundTransport`: no proxy, no redirects, a `ConnectCallback` that resolves the host, refuses unless every address is inside the endpoint's networks and outside the forbidden ranges (loopback outside vm-local, link-local and metadata, multicast, reserved, NAT64, 6to4, Teredo) and dials only a checked address (DNS rebinding), TLS against the system roots or the pinned CA with no certificate or revocation downloads; answers capped at 10 MB. `AcrisPoller` (Integration) claims due ACRIS endpoints (one replica each), pulls with `If-Modified-Since`, reads at most 5,000 flights strictly, maps them for the site (`AcrisMapping`, keys shared with AIDX through `FlightKeys`) and applies them as the feed `acris-` and the endpoint's code, recording status, Last-Modified and failures. Docs: integration.md, decisions, wiki 04, 08 (sections 9 and 11), 10 (4.8f), 11.
- Decisions: no proxy for outbound calls; no IHttpClientFactory or Polly (per-record transport rebuilt by version); integration permissions reused; the ACRIS profile follows the ACRIS Semantic Model names (To confirm per AODB); flight numbers lose leading zeros in keys (AIDX keys with zeros from earlier today change; no production data yet); built before the ARV-029 emulator with a stand-in AODB in the tests. Pending the product owner's approval: allowlist entries SEC-020 (`new HttpClient` in OutboundClients) and SEC-023 (`new SocketsHttpHandler` in OutboundTransport, a new scanner rule).
- Gates: unit tests (69: forbidden ranges incl. metadata, mapped, NAT64 and 6to4; registration rules; connection refusals and DNS rebinding; redirects; retries, breaker and trials; API key, HMAC, both token flows, token size; TLS with a pinned CA, another CA, no pin, wrong name, expired, self-signed and no AIA download; mutual TLS presented and refused certificates; the environment guard; the full chain against a loopback server; ACRIS reading and mapping); integration tests (administration within sites, secret protected at rest, moves need the secret, duplicate codes, kind fixed, versions, audit; the ACRIS pull against a loopback partner with Not Modified and a redirect endpoint; runtime role refusals); e2e `outbound-endpoints.spec.ts` (registration with a second factor, 9 unsafe connections refused, moving without the secret refused, the ACRIS pull against a stand-in AODB with its key, a redirect and an address outside the networks refused); the full unit (1855), integration (260) and e2e (330) suites; security scan 0 errors (5 pending warnings); docs.
- Security review (independent reviewer, CWE-918, CWE-306 and the baseline): FAIL on the first pass (four Medium, three Low and a process item), fixed: an administrator could move an endpoint to a host of theirs and receive its stored secret (now a move needs the secret); TLS chain building could download a certificate's AIA URL outside the guarded transport (now no downloads, one chain policy); no TLS, mutual TLS or environment-guard tests (added, the AIA test shown to fail without the fix); the lab settings checked only `Application:Environment` (now the host environment too); an unbounded token answer without a length; a refused or cancelled half-open trial left the circuit stuck; the scanner exemption was silent (now two allowlist entries pending approval and a new rule SEC-023). Also taken: the calling host rechecks HTTPS, clients built under a lock and replaced ones disposed with their certificate, concurrent pulls, 409 on a concurrent duplicate, only the trial call ends a trial, a secret replaced by a move audited. Re-review: PASS.

## 2026-10-03 ARV-046 SSIM import
- Summary: an administrator previews an IATA SSIM chapter 7 file for a site in Ariva.Api.Main (`api/v1/admin/sites/{siteCode}/flight-schedules/preview`, multipart, at most 20 MB, horizon 1 to 200 days, default 60) and imports exactly what was previewed (`.../import` with the preview's `previewToken`). `SsimReader` streams the file (200,000 lines of at most 200 printable ASCII characters, 100,000 expanded legs, SHA-256 while reading), reads the time mode of record 2 and the fields of each record 3, expands the legs touching the site's airports per operating date (days of operation, fortnightly, open periods, overnight arrivals) in UTC, checks every expanded leg with `FlightRules`, and reports bad lines by number with Ariva's reason only. The import goes through `ISvcFlightIntake.ApplyScheduleLegsAsync` as the fallback feed `ssim`: legs now record the feed that set their schedule and whether it was a fallback (script 0029); a fallback creates legs and updates only fallback-owned ones, a live feed always replaces a fallback's schedule. Units of 500, each its own transaction, rolled back on failure and never cancelled once the first is committed; the audit entry `FlightSchedule.Imported` is written in its own transaction with the outcome, units committed and counts. ACRIS poller units now roll back on failure too. Docs: integration.md, decisions, wiki 08 (SSIM), 10 (4.8g), 11.
- Decisions: preview token (Data Protection purpose `Ariva.FlightSchedules.Preview.v1`, file, site, horizon, window, user, two hours) instead of storing previews; ownership precedence instead of back-dated message times; horizon-bounded expansion; arrival dates derived from the times, not SSIM's date variation (To confirm); the time mode U arrival key uses the UTC departure date while AIDX keys by the origin's local date, so one flight near midnight could become two legs (To confirm before pilot); the FlightSchedule permission keeps all five actions, as the permission model requires, with only Create used.
- Gates: unit tests (SSIM expansion, time modes, fortnightly and open periods, malformed and rule-breaking records without echo, line limits at 200, 201 and 202 with every line ending, whole-file limits; leg ownership); integration tests (preview changes nothing; token refused for another file, horizon, user, site and after two hours; a live leg back-dated 40 days is unchanged; a live takeover wins and stays; a unit failing after its legs leaves exactly the committed units; the audit records failed and completed imports with the actor; the import runs to its end once a unit committed); e2e `flight-schedules.spec.ts` (preview then import, 409 for another file or horizon, 404 at another site, forged and missing tokens, line errors without echo, 413 one byte over 20 MB); the full unit (1879), integration (264) and e2e (334) suites; security scan 0 errors; docs.
- Security review (independent reviewer, CWE-120, CWE-501 and the baseline): FAIL on the first pass (four Medium, two Low), fixed: an import could overwrite a leg a live feed pre-loaded more than 29 days earlier (now ownership decides); an import failing part way left no audit entry and could be stopped half done (audit in its own transaction, units after the first not cancellable); a unit that threw was committed half applied on disposal (rolled back, also in the ACRIS poller); the preview was bound to the file only (now the token); the preview counted legs the intake would refuse and reflected their text (now checked); line limits one or two characters loose. Follow-ups taken: tests for the site and expiry parts of the token, commits never cancelled. Re-review: PASS.

## 2026-10-03 Fix: one unreadable ACRIS secret no longer stops the poll round
- The CI run of ARV-045 failed in `AcrisPull_Should_ApplyTheSitesFlightsAndRecordHowItWent_When_Due`: another test of the class had registered an ACRIS endpoint under a different test host, whose ephemeral key ring protected its secret, and the poller's `CryptographicException` for that endpoint failed the whole round. The same would happen in production after a key ring is lost or restored from an older backup. `AcrisPoller` now records "The endpoint's secret cannot be read with this key ring; set the secret again." for that endpoint (a failure, logged as an error) and pulls the others. The test registers an endpoint whose secret another ring protected, asserts it fails alone, and only its own endpoints are due. Runbook 4.8f updated. Gates: integration (264), docs, scan.

## 2026-10-03 ARV-029 AODB, AMAN and immigration emulators
- Summary: Ariva.Simulation.Api now plays the partner systems of the reference day on the sensor emulator's demo clock (`IDemoMinuteSink`, each on its own bounded queue; `FeedTime` lays demo minutes on real minutes). The AMAN emulator derives the four V1 contracts from the scenario (`AmanFeed`): desk sessions as desks open, pause and close (a run's first minute states every desk with a session), one-minute desk intervals (documents are people, transactions approaches of 1.25), e-gate intervals with rejects by coarse category and small cells under 3 folded into Other, and inbound lane demand two hours out and 30 minutes out with the reference split; AMAN's own codes (IN01 to IN22, EGIN1 to EGIN6, OUT01 to OUT22, EGOUT1 to EGOUT4) so Ariva's desk code mappings are exercised. It publishes on `aman.feed.*.v1` (Kafka, keyed, JSON as Ariva reads it) and through the immigration REST endpoints with its own integration client (token exchange, a fresh TOTP step per exchange, `X-TOTP-Code`, an `Idempotency-Key` per minute and contract). A mock immigration system sends the same contracts over REST only with its own client (departures by default). The AODB emulator (`AodbSchedule`) pushes AIDX 22.1 to Ariva (every leg at a run's first minute, then the legs whose estimate or block time changed) and serves ACRIS flights with an API key and `If-Modified-Since`. The mock AMAN Integration API (`aman/api/v1/auth`, `aman/api/v1/feed/{contract}`) is the partner for ARV-050. Controls under `api/v1/simulation/feeds`: status, AMAN codes, play a minute, load clients. Docs: Emulators/README.md, wiki 04, 08 (section 13), integration.md.
- Decisions: immigration REST batches will be `{ "items": [...] }` with `Idempotency-Key` like ARV-043 (ARV-048 builds the endpoints); AMAN and AODB share Ariva's flight key format; the mock AMAN API answers in AMAN's Result envelope (paths and paging To confirm against AMAN). Pending the product owner's approval: allowlist entry SEC-052 for `/aman/api/v1/auth`. Follow-ups: the simulator does not write `LogFile:Path`, so the e2e teardown cannot scan its logs; per-client lockout on the mock AMAN auth.
- Gates: unit tests (feed content against the scenario, determinism and data boundary, reject suppression, reference split, AIDX and ACRIS read and mapped by Ariva's own readers, settings validation incl. Kafka transport, TOTP parity with Ariva's, the integration client, the mock AMAN exchange, replay, expiry, 429, the ACRIS key, the queue bound, secrets never logged); integration tests (the emulator's AIDX applied by the real intake; the AMAN Kafka records read back by Ariva's Kafka value reader on a real broker); e2e `feed-emulators.spec.ts` (controls, the AODB's AIDX through its own client into Ariva, ACRIS with its key, the mock AMAN exchange and feed); the full unit (1918), integration (266) and e2e (344) suites; security scan 0 errors; docs.
- Security review (independent reviewer, CWE-287 and the baseline): FAIL on the first pass (four required), fixed: SASL credentials or plaintext Kafka to a remote broker were accepted (now SaslSsl for credentials, plaintext only to loopback or in-cluster); each feed's queue was unbounded (now 120 minutes, oldest dropped and counted); no test that secrets stay out of logs; no test of token expiry or of an unconfigured ACRIS key. Advisories taken: the TOTP check runs whatever the secret gave, settings tests, no token kept across a change of credentials, response bodies not kept, a per-key limit on play, the run's simulator secrets in the e2e log scan, and no call without an Ariva address. Re-review: PASS.

## 2026-10-03 ARV-048 AMAN feed and immigration endpoints
- Summary: the four AMAN feed contracts V1 (DeskSessionChanged, DeskIntervalStats, EGateIntervalStats, InboundFlightLaneDemand) enter Ariva through `ISvcImmigrationIntake` from two transports: AMAN's `aman.feed.*.v1` topics, consumed by Ariva.Api.Integration with the consume pipe (retries, an inbox keyed by SHA-256 of contract, site and source event id, dead letter), and the Integration API at `api/v1/integration/sites/{siteCode}/immigration/{desk-sessions|desk-interval-stats|egate-interval-stats|inbound-lane-demand}` (`immigration:write`, batches with `Idempotency-Key`, per-record results). `ImmigrationRules` checks each record (one-minute intervals on whole minutes, time window, sums, small-cell suppression, lanes, codes, source event ids) with Ariva's own reasons. The intake takes an explicit `ImmigrationScope` (the REST path's site, or for Kafka the record's known site), resolves codes through the site's AMAN desk code mappings whose desk is at the same site (unmapped codes kept apart with a warning), and stores each record once per site and source event id (script 0030; lane demand replaced only by a later computation; the runtime role cannot update or delete records). The demo seed maps AMAN's codes; the ARV-029 emulator now feeds these endpoints.
- Decisions: the immigration REST endpoints follow the Integration API batch pattern; Ariva.Core references Ariva.Business.Contracts (dependency-free); idempotency per site and source event id across both transports; both transports share strict converters (exact enum names as values and keys, times with an explicit offset). Open for the product owner: source event ids scoped per issuing system (decisions.md), a Kafka site allowlist; follow-up: rate-limit the Kafka refusal log.
- Gates: unit tests (rules against an emulator minute, each rule, overflow, exact enum names and keys on both transports, explicit offsets, round trip, idempotency operations against the SQL check); integration tests (a minute stored once over both transports with desks resolved, unmapped and wrong-kind codes, other and unknown sites, cross-site mappings, no echo, unchosen scope, lane demand ordering, runtime-role refusals and database checks); e2e (Kafka from the emulator, REST from AMAN and another immigration client, per-record checks, replay, refusals, scope, site and TOTP). Backend gate passed (unit 1952), integration 281 passed (1 pre-existing skip), e2e 353 passed, scan 0 errors, docs pass.
- Security review (independent reviewer, CWE-501, CWE-863 and the baseline): FAIL on the first pass (four required), fixed: two reject categories at int.MaxValue overflowed the sum (a 500 on REST, retries on Kafka); `JsonStringEnumConverter` accepted other cases, padding and comma-joined names (OR-ed into another member) as values and keys; a refused record's raw desk or gate code was echoed as its key; a desk code mapping could resolve to another site's desk. Advisories taken: explicit scope instead of a null site, per-site unmapped log cap, mappings loaded once per site, explicit offsets, more runtime-role tests, doc fixes. Re-review PASS.
- Also: the ARV-029 notes had landed on ARV-047 in the PRD; moved back.

## 2026-10-03 Fix: emulator clients allow the IPv6 loopback in E2E
- The CI run of ARV-029 failed in `the emulated AODB pushes the demo schedule`: every token exchange of the simulator's client answered 401. The simulator calls Ariva directly on `localhost`, which resolves to `::1` on the CI runner (IPv6 up) and to 127.0.0.1 in the build container, and the client allowed only 127.0.0.0/8 and 10.0.0.0/8, so the exchange came from outside its networks. The emulator clients of `feed-emulators.spec.ts` and `aman-feed.spec.ts` now also allow `::1/128`. The two specs pass locally (8); the IPv6 path is confirmed only by the next CI run.

## 2026-10-03 ARV-047 Arrival-wave projection
- Summary: `ArrivalWave` (Ariva.Core, pure, formulas F14) projects a site's arriving legs into hall arrivals per minute and lane: T_f is the on-block, else the later of landing plus taxi-in and the estimate, else the estimate, else the schedule (floored to the minute); passengers and split from AMAN's `InboundFlightLaneDemand` (ARV-048) when received, else the feed's passenger estimate or seats times 0.8 with the default mix (`Flights:ArrivalWave`, checked at start); passengers reach the hall from T_f + d (11, 8 to 15) over 12 minutes with the reference weights; the alert window sums now + 5 to now + 25. Ariva.Api.Main serves `GET api/v1/sites/{siteCode}/arrival-wave?minutes=` (5 to 120, default 30; parsed by hand so a bad value is never echoed) with the flights landing in the window and those still arriving, the curve and a `truncated` flag (`ArrivingLegs`, parameterised SQL, at most 2,000 legs). New permissions `ArrivalWave.View` (border shift supervisor, terminal duty manager, administrator) and `ArrivalWaveLanes.View` (border shift supervisor, administrator): airport roles get totals only, the split being border data. `ProjectedArrivalWave` replaces `NoArrivalWave` for predicted-breach rules (Stream evaluation and Main backtest): a queue zone gets the lanes of the desks its service zones stand at, shared among the queue zones serving the same lane.
- Decisions: recorded in decisions.md (precedence, Proposed taxi-in and load factor, one delay and mix per deployment, zone lanes through service zones, no stored projections). Open for the product owner: queue zones declaring their lane categories (the demo profile links no desks, so predicted rules at DMO still have nothing to judge); whether airport roles may see AMAN's per-flight totals. The web strip is not built (no Ariva.Web screen consumes operational APIs yet).
- Gates: unit tests (F14's two tests, precedence, sources, window edges, floored in-block, alert window, settings and binding); integration tests (projection with AMAN and mix, seats, no passengers, cancelled and departures left out, window 60, unknown site, bad window, a site outside the caller's sites, totals for a terminal duty manager, two sites sharing a flight key, the zone source's lane sharing, overflow, a zone without lanes and the past); e2e `arrival-wave.spec.ts` (legs and lane demand through the Integration API, supervisor and terminal views, 401, 403, 404, bad windows without echo). Backend gate passed (unit 1961), integration 284 passed (1 pre-existing skip), e2e 355 passed (1 flaky: the AIDX oversize test's upload hit EPIPE and passed on retry), scan 0 errors, docs pass.
- Security review (independent reviewer, CWE-863 and the baseline): FAIL on the first pass (no test proved at the data level that one site's flights or lane demand cannot reach another's), fixed with the two-sites test; advisories taken: lane split as border data, a site check in the service as well as the endpoint, the backtest wording, the truncation flag, wiki 11. Re-review PASS.
- Also: the CI run of the IPv6 loopback fix (d864ca9) passed, confirming the cause.

## 2026-10-03 ARV-049 Desk sessions and e-gate coupling
- Summary: `DeskFeed` (Ariva.Api.Stream, every `Border:DeskFeed:PollSeconds`) reads each site's AMAN records as the immigration intake stored them (ARV-048; by receipt time from an `AmanFeedCursor` with a two-minute overlap for late commits, leaving out the records already taken, at most `MaxRead` per read and `2 x MaxRead` remembered) and turns them into desk signals at their F10 rank (`AmanDeskFeed`: session changes rank 2, interval transactions rank 1 as activity), with AMAN's feed as the heartbeat of every AMAN desk (never past the host's clock), for one `DeskStateEngine` per site; closed minutes go to `desk_minute`, AMAN's e-gate intervals to `egate_minute`, and the engine snapshot, read position and heartbeat to `desk_feed_state` (script 0031) in the same transaction under a per-site advisory lock (class 49). Keys are site/checkpoint/desk; desks sharing a key are left out and logged. `EgateCoupling` (F12) adds the e-gate rejects to their manual lane (`Border:EgateCoupling:RejectLane`, VIS) after the lag in the projection predicted-breach rules read (r measured over 30 minutes from AMAN's mapped gates, else 0.07); the arrival-wave API shows r and the lane to border roles.
- Decisions: read the stored records rather than AMAN's topics or new events (one validation, mapping and idempotency path); the feed is the AMAN desks' heartbeat (F10's T_stale on the feed), so a desk with no session is Closed while AMAN lives and Unknown when it stops. Open: the nowcast's desk term (changes stored stream outputs; needs zones linked to lanes), staff and service zone readings into the desk engine, per-site reject lane.
- Gates: unit tests (rank mapping, heartbeats per minute and their cap, a closed desk kept closed by the feed and Unknown after it stops, Paused, the cursor's overlap, bursts larger than a read, the remembered-records floor, ahead-dated records, F12's coupling test, reject rate measured or reference, settings); integration tests (an XS2 site with AMAN desks: open, closed and never-reported desks minute by minute, transactions counted once, e-gate minutes, Unknown after AMAN stops, another replica's lock, DMO's records never reaching XS2, a 250-record burst read in steps of 100, the rejects joining the visitors' projected demand, runtime role refused on `desk_feed_state`). Backend gate passed (unit 1970), integration 287 passed (1 pre-existing skip), e2e 355 passed with the desk feed running in Stream (1 known flaky: the skip-link test), scan 0 errors, docs pass.
- Security review (independent reviewer, baseline): FAIL on the first pass (a burst larger than one read could stick a site's read position for good; one ahead-dated record could silence the heartbeat for a day; no tests for the new table's runtime privileges or for site separation), fixed with tests; advisories taken: per-site failure isolation, state size checked in SQL, shared keys refused, unmapped gates left out of r with an index, the reset keeping same-instant records, runbook 4.8i. Re-review PASS; open advisories: the taken-id comparison cost during a large burst, heartbeat volume above about 3,300 desks per site, per-row e-gate upserts.

## 2026-10-03 Fix: configuration binding changed the reference lane mix
- The CI run of ARV-047 failed in `Split_Should_RemoveTransfersAndTakeTheEgateShare_When_FormulasF14ReferenceMix` with another mix's numbers: `ArrivalWaveSettings.Mix` defaulted to the shared `LaneMix.Reference` instance, and configuration binding sets the properties of the instance it finds, so binding a configured mix (the settings test, or a deployment's `Flights:ArrivalWave:Mix`) rewrote the reference for the whole process. Locally the tests ran in an order that hid it. `LaneMix.Reference` and `ArrivalWaveSettings.Default` now give a new instance each time; the settings test checks the reference after binding. Unit tests pass (all).

## 2026-10-03 ARV-050 Outbound AMAN connector
